# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V2.1 | PAGE 4 OF 8

## A.20 Executive Summary

### A.20.1 Executive summary

No additional funds are required for these recommendations as they are being implemented through the existing Green Climate Fund (GCF) and other climate finance mechanisms. The GCF is a new and innovative financing mechanism that was established in 2010 to support developing countries in their efforts to address climate change. The GCF has already mobilized over $10 billion for climate action, with a significant portion of this funding going towards projects in the most vulnerable regions of the world.

### A.21 Executive summary

1. **The Great American Dry Corridor and the And Zones of the Dominican Republic are among the world's most vulnerable to climate change.** The region is experiencing increased frequency and intensity of droughts, floods, and other extreme weather events due to climate change. These events have led to significant economic losses and social disruption in the region.

2. **The impact of these events has been exacerbated by the lack of adequate infrastructure and preparedness.** The region's infrastructure is not designed to withstand the increased frequency and intensity of extreme weather events, which has led to significant damage and loss of life. Additionally, the region's preparedness for climate change is limited, with few resources available to support early warning systems and disaster response.

3. **Climate change is expected to continue to impact the region in the coming years.** The Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC) has projected that the region will experience increased temperatures of 1-2°C by 2050, with a corresponding increase in the frequency and intensity of extreme weather events. This will have significant implications for the region's agriculture, water resources, and infrastructure.

4. **The region is also facing significant economic challenges.** The region's economy is heavily dependent on agriculture, which is highly vulnerable to climate change. Additionally, the region has a high level of poverty and inequality, which exacerbates the impact of climate change on the most vulnerable populations.

5. **The region is in need of additional funding to support its climate change efforts.** While the GCF provides significant funding for climate action, the region's needs are expected to exceed the available resources. Additional funding will be required to support the implementation of climate change adaptation and mitigation projects in the region.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2.1 | PAGE 7 OF 8

## PROJECT/PROGRAMME INFORMATION

### 5. Climate services

#### 5.1 Climate services

The DR Corridor of Central America consists of eight and sub-humid ecosystems that cover most of the region. The climate is tropical with two seasons: dry (December to April) and rainy (May to November). The project area includes the Dominican Republic, Haiti, Panama, Nicaragua, Honduras, El Salvador, Guatemala, Belize, Costa Rica, and southern Mexico. The total population in these countries is approximately 42 million people. Climate change impacts are already affecting the region's ecosystems and communities.

#### 5.2 Project summary

The project aims to enhance climate resilience by improving the capacity of national meteorological services (NMS) to provide climate information and early warning systems. This will enable governments, businesses, and communities to better prepare for extreme weather events and reduce their vulnerability to climate change. The project will also support regional cooperation and integration among NMS in the region.

#### 5.3 Project objectives

- Improve the capacity of national meteorological services (NMS) to provide accurate and timely climate information.
- Enhance early warning systems to better prepare for extreme weather events.
- Strengthen regional cooperation and integration among NMS in Central America.
- Increase public awareness and understanding of climate change impacts and adaptation measures.

#### 5.4 Project activities

- Conduct training workshops for meteorologists and other relevant staff at NMS.
- Provide technical assistance to improve the quality and reliability of climate data.
- Develop and implement early warning systems for extreme weather events.
- Establish regional networks for information sharing and collaboration among NMS.
- Conduct public awareness campaigns to promote understanding of climate change impacts and adaptation measures.

#### 5.5 Project outcomes

- Improved capacity of national meteorological services (NMS) to provide accurate and timely climate information.
- Enhanced early warning systems that better prepare communities for extreme weather events.
- Strengthened regional cooperation and integration among NMS in Central America.
- Increased public awareness and understanding of climate change impacts and adaptation measures.

#### 5.6 Project budget

The total project budget is $10 million, with the following allocation:

- Technical assistance: $4 million
- Training workshops: $2 million
- Early warning systems: $3 million
- Regional networks: $0.5 million
- Public awareness campaigns: $0.5 million

#### 5.7 Project timeline

The project will be implemented over a period of five years, with the following milestones:

- Year 1: Conduct needs assessment and develop project plan.
- Years 2-3: Implement training workshops and technical assistance.
- Year 4: Develop and implement early warning systems.
- Year 5: Establish regional networks and conduct public awareness campaigns.

#### 5.8 Project location

The project will be implemented in the following countries:

- Dominican Republic
- Haiti
- Panama
- Nicaragua
- Honduras
- El Salvador
- Guatemala
- Belize
- Costa Rica
- Southern Mexico

#### 5.9 Project partners

The project is implemented by a consortium of international and regional organizations, including:

- World Meteorological Organization (WMO)
- United Nations Environment Programme (UNEP)
- Pan American Health Organization (PAHO)
- Inter-American Development Bank (IDB)

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2.1 | PAGE 9 OF 8

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a new climate fund under the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). The GCF was established to support projects and programs in developing countries aimed at mitigating and adapting to climate change.

## Objective
The objective of the GCF is to promote a low-emission, climate-resilient development pathway by providing support to developing countries to limit or reduce their greenhouse gas emissions and to adapt to the impacts of climate change. The GCF aims to mobilize $100 billion per year by 2020 from a wide range of public and private sources.

## Funding Mechanism
The GCF operates through two funding windows: the Mitigation Window and the Adaptation Window. The Mitigation Window focuses on projects that reduce greenhouse gas emissions, while the Adaptation Window supports projects that help communities adapt to the impacts of climate change.

### Mitigation Window
The Mitigation Window provides support for projects that aim to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by promoting renewable energy, energy efficiency, and sustainable transport. Examples of projects funded through this window include:

- Renewable energy projects such as wind farms and solar power plants.
- Energy efficiency projects in buildings and industries.
- Sustainable transport projects like electric buses and bike-sharing programs.

### Adaptation Window
The Adaptation Window supports projects that help communities adapt to the impacts of climate change, such as:

- Water management projects to address issues like droughts and floods.
- Early warning systems for natural disasters.
- Climate-resilient infrastructure projects like sea walls and flood defenses.

## Governance Structure
The GCF is governed by a Board composed of 24 members, with an equal number of representatives from developed and developing countries. The Board makes decisions on funding proposals and sets the overall direction of the Fund.

### Board Composition
- **Developed Country Representatives:** 12 members
- **Developing Country Representatives:** 12 members

The Board is supported by various committees, including the Standing Committee on Finance (SCF), which provides technical advice and recommendations to the Board.

## Funding Process
The funding process involves several steps:

1. **Project Identification:** Potential projects are identified through a call for proposals or direct access.
2. **Proposal Submission:** Eligible entities submit their project proposals to the GCF.
3. **Evaluation:** Proposals are evaluated based on criteria such as climate impact, sustainability, and co-financing.
4. **Approval:** The Board reviews and approves projects that meet the evaluation criteria.
5. **Funding Disbursement:** Approved projects receive funding from the GCF.

## Key Documents
- **GCF Readiness and Preparatory Support Programme (RPS):** Provides support to developing countries to prepare for GCF funding.
- **GCF Investment Funds:** Managed by accredited entities, these funds provide additional financing options for climate projects.
- **GCF Accreditation Manual:** Outlines the process for accrediting entities that can access GCF funding.

## Conclusion
The Green Climate Fund plays a crucial role in supporting developing countries to mitigate and adapt to climate change. Through its structured governance, funding windows, and rigorous evaluation process, the GCF ensures that projects are aligned with global climate goals and contribute to sustainable development.

## Climate Change Impacts on Agriculture
Climate change impacts on agriculture are expected to have severe implications for food security and poverty reduction. In sub-Saharan Africa, climate change is projected to impact agriculture negatively through changes in precipitation patterns and temperature increases. These changes will affect crop yields and livestock productivity, leading to reduced agricultural output.

### Drought Impacts
Droughts are one of the most significant impacts of climate change on agriculture. Droughts can lead to crop failures and livestock deaths, resulting in food shortages and economic losses. The frequency and severity of droughts are expected to increase due to climate change, exacerbating existing vulnerabilities in agricultural systems.

### Temperature Changes
Rising temperatures can have both positive and negative effects on agriculture. Warmer temperatures can extend the growing season and allow for the cultivation of new crops. However, excessive heat can stress plants and reduce yields. In some regions, increased temperatures may also lead to the spread of pests and diseases, further impacting agricultural productivity.

### Precipitation Changes
Changes in precipitation patterns can significantly affect agriculture. Increased rainfall can improve soil moisture and support crop growth, while reduced rainfall can lead to drought conditions. Climate change is expected to alter precipitation patterns, leading to more frequent and severe weather events such as floods and storms. These changes can disrupt agricultural activities and reduce crop yields.

### Regional Impacts
The impacts of climate change on agriculture vary by region. In sub-Saharan Africa, for example, climate change is projected to reduce crop yields and increase the frequency of droughts. In South Asia, increased temperatures and altered precipitation patterns are expected to affect rice production. In Central America, changes in temperature and rainfall patterns are likely to impact coffee and banana production.

### Policy Responses
Governments and international organizations are developing policies to address the impacts of climate change on agriculture. These policies include investments in climate-smart agriculture, early warning systems for extreme weather events, and improved agricultural practices. Additionally, efforts are being made to enhance resilience and adaptability in agricultural systems through research and development.

### References
- Patz, J. A., O'Neill, B. S., Leiserowitz, A., Rosenthal, D., Lemos, M., et al. (2014). Climate change impacts in Central America: a high-resolution assessment.
- IPCC (2014). Climate Change 2014: Impacts, Adaptation, and Vulnerability. Part A: Global and Sectoral Aspects. Contribution of Working Group II to the Fifth Assessment Report of the Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2 | PAGE 13 OF 18

## Green Climate Fund Proposal

### Executive Summary

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) proposal is designed to support climate change mitigation and adaptation projects in the Pacific Islands Region. The project aims to enhance climate resilience, reduce greenhouse gas emissions, and promote sustainable development.

#### Objectives
- **Mitigation**: Reduce greenhouse gas emissions through renewable energy adoption.
- **Adaptation**: Enhance climate resilience by improving infrastructure and early warning systems.
- **Sustainable Development**: Promote economic growth while protecting the environment.

### Project Description

The project will be implemented in five Pacific Island countries: Fiji, Solomon Islands, Tonga, and Vanuatu. The project aims to:

1. **Reduce Emissions**: Implement renewable energy projects such as solar and wind farms.
2. **Enhance Resilience**: Build climate-resilient infrastructure including sea walls and early warning systems.
3. **Promote Sustainable Practices**: Support sustainable agriculture and forestry practices.

### Project Components

The project includes the following components:

1. **Renewable Energy**: Installation of solar panels and wind turbines.
2. **Climate-Resilient Infrastructure**: Construction of sea walls, drainage systems, and early warning systems.
3. **Sustainable Agriculture**: Promotion of climate-smart agriculture practices.

### Project Implementation

The project will be implemented over a five-year period with the following milestones:

1. **Year 1**: Conduct baseline studies and develop project plans.
2. **Years 2-4**: Implement renewable energy projects, climate-resilient infrastructure, and sustainable agriculture initiatives.
3. **Year 5**: Monitor and evaluate project outcomes.

### Project Budget

The total budget for the project is $50 million, with funding from the Green Climate Fund and co-financing from national governments.

### Project Management

The project will be managed by a regional coordinating unit based in Suva, Fiji. The unit will oversee implementation across all five countries and ensure coordination among stakeholders.

### Conclusion

The Green Climate Fund proposal aims to support sustainable development while enhancing climate resilience and reducing greenhouse gas emissions in the Pacific Islands Region.

## The Green Climate Fund (GCF) and its Relevance to the Paris Agreement

The GCF was established in 2010 as a financial mechanism under the UN Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). It aims to support developing countries in mitigating and adapting to climate change. The GCF has been operational since 2015, with a total pledged amount of $10.3 billion.

The methods from the analysis include:

- **GCF Accreditation**: This involves the accreditation process for entities that can access GCF funds.
- **GCF Funding**: This includes the different types of funding available through the GCF, such as grants and co-financing.

## INTRODUCTION

### Overview
This proposal outlines the Green Climate Fund (GCF) funding request for climate change mitigation and adaptation projects. The GCF aims to support developing countries in their efforts to address climate change.

### Objectives
1. Mitigate greenhouse gas emissions.
2. Enhance resilience to climate change impacts.
3. Promote low-emission and climate-resilient development pathways.

## OBJECTIVES

| Category                | Objective                                                                 |
|-------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Mitigation              | Reduce greenhouse gas emissions through various initiatives.            |
| Adaptation              | Build resilience to climate change impacts in vulnerable communities.    |
| Capacity Building       | Strengthen institutional capacities for effective climate action.         |

## BENEFICIARIES

| Group                    | Description                                                                   |
|--------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Developing Countries     | Nations most affected by climate change and least responsible for emissions. |
| Local Communities        | Vulnerable populations requiring immediate support.                         |
| Private Sector           | Businesses adopting sustainable practices.                                  |

## IMPLEMENTATION

### Activities
1. **Renewable Energy Projects**: Transition to clean energy sources.
2. **Energy Efficiency Programs**: Improve efficiency in various sectors.
3. **Climate Resilience Initiatives**: Enhance resilience of vulnerable communities.

### Timeline
- **Phase 1 (Year 1)**: Initial planning and project setup.
- **Phase 2 (Years 2-5)**: Implementation of key activities.
- **Phase 3 (Year 6)**: Monitoring, evaluation, and reporting.

## BUDGET

| Item                      | Cost Estimate |
|---------------------------|---------------|
| Renewable Energy Projects  | $10 million   |
| Energy Efficiency Programs| $8 million    |
| Climate Resilience Initiatives | $5 million   |

### Funding Sources
- **GCF Contribution**: $23 million.
- **Co-financing**: $7 million from national and international partners.

## FIGURE 6: CHANGES IN HYDROLOGICAL VARIABLES BETWEEN PREINDUSTRIAL AND PRESENT DAY CLIMATE

### Description
This figure illustrates the changes in various hydrological variables between preindustrial times and present-day climate. The data highlights significant shifts due to human activities.

### Key Points
- **Sea Level Rise**: Significant increase due to melting of ice caps.
- **Precipitation Patterns**: Changes in rainfall distribution affecting agricultural productivity.
- **Temperature Increase**: Impact on ecosystems and biodiversity.

## SECTION 24: ECONOMIC ANALYSIS OF THE BENEFITS OF LAND RESTORATION

### Introduction
Land restoration provides numerous economic benefits, including enhanced agricultural productivity, improved water quality, and increased carbon sequestration. This section analyzes the economic impact of land restoration initiatives.

### Benefits
1. **Increased Agricultural Yield**: Improved soil health leads to higher crop yields.
2. **Water Quality Improvement**: Reduced runoff and erosion enhance water quality.
3. **Carbon Sequestration**: Enhanced carbon storage mitigates climate change.

### Conclusion
Land restoration is a cost-effective strategy for achieving multiple environmental and economic goals, supporting sustainable development objectives.

## SECTION 25: THE IMPACT OF TECHNOLOGY ON CLIMATE CHANGE MITIGATION

### Introduction
Technological advancements play a crucial role in mitigating climate change. This section explores the impact of various technologies on reducing greenhouse gas emissions and enhancing resilience to climate impacts.

### Key Technologies
1. **Renewable Energy Technologies**: Solar, wind, and hydro power.
2. **Energy Storage Solutions**: Batteries and other storage systems.
3. **Carbon Capture and Storage (CCS)**: Reducing emissions from industrial processes.

### Conclusion
Investment in advanced technologies is essential for achieving climate goals and transitioning to a low-carbon economy.

## Introduction

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is implementing its first adaptation results-based financing program. Furthermore, the proposed program will be implemented in collaboration with the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP). The program aims to support small-scale projects that are implemented by local governments and communities. These projects aim to enhance climate resilience and reduce greenhouse gas emissions.

### 31. Addition to CAHUBU 02

The proposed program will align with the Green Climate Fund's (GCF) project cycle, which includes the following steps:

- **Identification of potential projects**
- **Preparation of project proposals**
- **Submission of project proposals to GCF**
- **Approval and implementation of projects**

### 32. Sewerage and sanitation projects

Several national projects are being implemented in Central America with a focus on waste management, water treatment, and sewerage systems. These projects aim to improve the quality of life for communities by reducing environmental impacts.

Examples include:

- **Sewerage system construction**
- **Wastewater treatment plants**
- **Water treatment facilities**

These projects are implemented in collaboration with local governments and communities. The projects aim to reduce environmental impacts and improve public health.

### 33. National Adaptation Plan (NAP)

The NAP process is a nationally led effort to identify the needs of countries to reduce vulnerability and adapt to the effects of climate change. The NAP process is implemented in collaboration with UNDP and other partners.

Examples include:

- **Climate risk assessments**
- **Development of adaptation strategies**
- **Implementation of adaptation measures**

The projects aim to enhance the resilience of communities to the impacts of climate change.

- **Identification of needs**
- **Development of strategies**
- **Implementation of measures**

The projects aim to enhance the resilience of communities to the impacts of climate change.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2 | PAGE 19 OF 88

## Description of the problem

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a unique financial mechanism to assist developing countries and vulnerable societies in addressing climate change. The GCF was established by 194 countries at the Durban Conference in December 2011, with the aim of mobilizing $100 billion per year by 2020 for mitigation and adaptation projects in developing countries. However, despite its noble intentions, the GCF has faced numerous challenges that have hindered its effectiveness.

One significant challenge is the lack of awareness about climate change impacts among local communities. Many people are unaware of the consequences of climate change and how it affects their daily lives. This lack of knowledge makes it difficult for them to take appropriate action to mitigate or adapt to climate change.

Another challenge is the lack of access to funding. Despite the GCF's goal of mobilizing $100 billion per year, many developing countries still struggle to access the funds they need to implement climate change projects. This is often due to complex and bureaucratic processes that make it difficult for local communities to apply for and receive funding.

Additionally, there is a lack of capacity among local communities to implement climate change projects. Many people lack the skills and knowledge needed to design, implement, and monitor effective climate change projects. This lack of capacity can lead to ineffective projects that do not achieve their intended goals.

## Preferred adaptation solution

The preferred adaptation solution for the Green Climate Fund is to improve the climate resilience of local communities by providing them with access to funding and technical assistance. This solution involves several key components:

1. **Awareness Raising**: Increase awareness about climate change impacts among local communities through education and outreach programs.
2. **Access to Funding**: Simplify the process for accessing GCF funds, making it easier for local communities to apply for and receive funding.
3. **Capacity Building**: Provide training and technical assistance to help local communities design, implement, and monitor effective climate change projects.

By implementing these components, the Green Climate Fund can enhance its effectiveness in assisting developing countries and vulnerable societies in addressing climate change.

## Barriers to the preferred adaptation solution

There are several barriers that hinder the implementation of the preferred adaptation solution for the Green Climate Fund:

1. **Limited Knowledge and Understanding**: Many local communities lack a comprehensive understanding of climate change impacts, making it difficult for them to take appropriate action.
2. **Complex Application Process**: The process for accessing GCF funds is complex and bureaucratic, making it challenging for local communities to apply for and receive funding.
3. **Lack of Capacity**: Local communities often lack the skills and knowledge needed to design, implement, and monitor effective climate change projects.

To overcome these barriers, the Green Climate Fund needs to invest in awareness raising, simplify the application process, and provide capacity building support to local communities.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.2 | PAGE 1 OF 8

## 3. Green Climate Fund Proposal

A request for a no-strings attached grant is being made to the Green Environmental Observatory based at the Central University of Technology (CUT) in Bloemfontein, Free State Province. The proposal aims to support the development and implementation of climate change adaptation and mitigation projects in South Africa.

### 3.1 Objectives

The objectives are:

- To ensure the sustainability of programme interventions in addressing these matters.
- To enhance the capacity of the Green Environmental Observatory to implement its mandate.
- To ensure that the Green Environmental Observatory is able to deliver on its objectives through the support of this proposal.
- To contribute to a more comprehensive understanding of climate change impacts and adaptation measures.

### 3.2 Background

The Green Environmental Observatory was established in 2015 as part of the CUT's commitment to addressing environmental challenges. The observatory has been involved in various research projects, including:

- Assessing the impact of climate change on water resources.
- Developing strategies for sustainable urban planning.
- Conducting research on renewable energy technologies.

### 3.3 Proposed Activities

The proposed activities include:

#### 3.3.1 Research and Development

- Conduct research on the impacts of climate change in South Africa, with a focus on vulnerable communities.
- Develop and implement adaptation measures to mitigate the effects of climate change.

#### 3.3.2 Capacity Building

- Provide training and capacity building for local communities and stakeholders.
- Develop educational materials and resources to raise awareness about climate change.

### 3.4 Budget

The budget for this proposal is outlined in Table 1 below:

| Item                          | Cost (ZAR) |
|-------------------------------|-----------|
| Research and Development     | 500,000   |
| Capacity Building             | 200,000   |
| Equipment                     | 150,000   |
| Travel and Accommodation      | 100,000   |
| Contingency                    | 50,000    |
| **Total**                      | **950,000** |

### 3.5 Implementation

The proposed activities will be implemented over a period of two years, with the following milestones:

- **Month 1-3**: Conduct initial research and develop a detailed project plan.
- **Month 4-6**: Implement adaptation measures and begin capacity building initiatives.
- **Month 7-9**: Continue implementation and monitor progress.
- **Month 10-12**: Evaluate the impact of the interventions and prepare a final report.

### 3.6 Conclusion

The Green Environmental Observatory at CUT is well-positioned to deliver on its objectives through this proposal. The observatory has a strong track record in research and capacity building, and is committed to addressing the challenges posed by climate change.

## Objective and Transparent practices

This will ensure **Barrier B**. The activities described above will ensure that the GCF resources are used to support the development of climate-resilient infrastructure, including renewable energy systems for climate change response planning and development in the seven target countries.

### 4.1. Together with the climate-affected interventions well overcome the barriers by improving (single reference of our work) and strengthening the capacity of local governments to implement the Theory of Change Disaster Resilience and Adaptation (ToCDRA). The goal is to enhance climate resilience of vulnerable communities through the implementation of the following activities:

1. **Climate change risk assessment** - Identify and assess climate-related hazards and their potential impacts on the project area.
2. **Long-term community development** - Implement sustainable development initiatives that address the root causes of vulnerability.

## Theories of Change

### Theory 1
- **Objective**: Enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities to climate change.
- **Activities**:
  - Conduct climate risk assessments.
  - Develop and implement adaptation strategies.
  - Build the capacity of local governments.

### Theory 2
- **Objective**: Improve access to climate-resilient infrastructure for vulnerable communities.
- **Activities**:
  - Identify critical infrastructure needs.
  - Design and construct climate-resilient infrastructure.
  - Monitor and evaluate the effectiveness of interventions.

### Theory 3
- **Objective**: Strengthen the capacity of local governments to manage climate risks.
- **Activities**:
  - Provide training and technical assistance.
  - Develop and implement climate risk management plans.
  - Foster partnerships with other stakeholders.

## Page 8 of Theory of Change diagram

### 5.3. Programming zone allocation
42. Programming interventions will be implemented in all ten countries of the Dry Corridor – Costa Rica, El Salvador, Guatemala, Honduras, Nicaragua, Panama, and the Dominican Republic. The large investment has been selected for each country based on a baseline climate change vulnerability assessment. Specifically, the following countries have been identified as priority areas:
- **Guatemala**: Chimaltenango, Quetzaltenango, Totonicapán.
- **Honduras**: Francisco Morazán, Yoro, Atlántida.
- **Nicaragua**: Boaco, Matagalpa, Jinotega.

These zones have been identified based on their vulnerability to climate change and the need for enhanced resilience measures. The interventions will focus on building climate-resilient infrastructure, enhancing community preparedness, and promoting sustainable practices in agriculture, water management, and disaster risk reduction.

## G

- **Project Title:** Green Climate Fund Proposal for Ecosystem Restoration and Sustainable Development in the San Miguel Region.
- **Location:**
  - **Country:** Mexico
  - **States:** Guanajuato, Jalisco, Michoacán, Puebla, Querétaro, Tlaxcala
  - **Cities/Towns:** El Salto, Guanajuato; San Miguel el Alto, El Salto; Atotonilco el Grande, San Miguel el Alto; San Juan del Río, Querétaro; Huimilpan, Querétaro; San Francisco del Rincón, Guanajuato.

## Project Description

The project aims to restore and protect ecosystems in the San Miguel region by implementing sustainable practices. The project will create an enabling environment for sustainable and sustainable development through:

- **Restoration of degraded lands** (10,000 hectares).
- **Improvement of water management** systems.
- **Promotion of renewable energy** sources.
- **Capacity building** for local communities.

The project will be implemented over a period of 5 years with an estimated budget of USD 2 million. The project will contribute to the achievement of the following SDGs:

- **SDG 1:** No Poverty
- **SDG 6:** Clean Water and Sanitation
- **SDG 7:** Affordable and Clean Energy
- **SDG 13:** Climate Action

The NDA will facilitate the implementation of projects and programs that are consistent with the country's priorities and needs as well as the GCF's strategic objectives. The NDA will also ensure that the GCF resources are used effectively, efficiently and transparently.

- Will approve funds received in sub-national or regional EA systems on behalf of beneficiaries;
- Will ensure that projects approved under its accreditation are implemented in accordance with the GCF policies and procedures;
- Will ensure that project proposals meet the requirements for accreditation and funding;
- Will ensure that the NDA is accountable to the government, parliament, and other stakeholders.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2.1 | PAGE 37 OF 88

## Introduction

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a unique financial mechanism established to support developing countries in their efforts to address climate change. The GCF aims to promote a paradigm shift towards low-emission and climate-resilient development pathways by providing support to mitigation and adaptation projects and programs in developing countries.

### Grant Facility

#### Overview

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) Grant Facility provides funding for projects that aim to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and enhance resilience to the adverse effects of climate change. The facility supports a wide range of activities, including capacity building, technology transfer, and knowledge sharing.

#### Objectives

- **Objective 1:** Strengthen the adaptive capacity of vulnerable countries through enhanced implementation of ESA and other climate-related technologies.
- **Objective 2:** Enhance resilience to climate change by promoting climate-smart agriculture and sustainable land management practices.
- **Objective 3:** Support the development and deployment of renewable energy technologies in developing countries.

#### Outputs

- **Output 1.1:** Implement landscape ESA interventions within rural communities across the seven target countries.
- **Output 1.2:** Implement demonstration water and resource-efficient technologies within rural communities.
- **Output 1.3:** Establish links with local governments to support sustainable selection and promotion of local ESA activities.

#### Output 2 Information on climate change adaptation and its financing, disseminated across the region and mainstreamed into national policies.

**SA:** This component will be aligned with the ongoing work that seeks to promote the regional strategy for Environmental Management and the regional strategy for Climate Change. The regional strategy for Environmental Management is being implemented through the Regional Environment Programme (REP) of the Caribbean Community (CARICOM). The regional strategy for Climate Change is being implemented through the Caribbean Regional Framework for Achieving Development Resilient Islands (CFRF-AIDI).

#### Output 3 Information on climate change adaptation and its financing, disseminated across the region and mainstreamed into national policies.

**SA:** This component will be aligned with the ongoing work that seeks to promote the regional strategy for Environmental Management and the regional strategy for Climate Change. The regional strategy for Environmental Management is being implemented through the Regional Environment Programme (REP) of the Caribbean Community (CARICOM). The regional strategy for Climate Change is being implemented through the Caribbean Regional Framework for Achieving Development Resilient Islands (CFRF-AIDI).

## Objective of ESA in the Dry Corridor

- Hub 3.1.2: Access to climate information for the implementation of CCH, including the use of mobile phones and other communication technologies.

### Component 3: Financing and implementation of ESA as well as water- and resource-efficient technologies across selected municipalities

#### Sub-component 3.1: Financing of ESAs through FAO and FAD

The sub-component will contribute to the objective of the project, with the distribution of funds from FAO and FAD, which will be used for the implementation of ESA in the Dry Corridor. The sub-component is focused on the following activities:

- Activities 3.1.1: FAO will be responsible for the management of the funds.
- Activities 3.1.2: FAD will be responsible for the execution of the project.

#### Sub-component 3.2: Implementation of ESA in the Dry Corridor

The sub-component will contribute to the objective of the project, with the implementation of ESA in the Dry Corridor. The sub-component is focused on the following activities:

- Activities 3.2.1: Implementation of ESA.
- Activities 3.2.2: Monitoring and evaluation of ESA.

#### Sub-component 3.3: Promotion of water- and resource-efficient technologies

The sub-component will contribute to the objective of the project, with the promotion of water- and resource-efficient technologies. The sub-component is focused on the following activities:

- Activities 3.3.1: Promotion of water- and resource-efficient technologies.
- Activities 3.3.2: Training for farmers in the use of water- and resource-efficient technologies.

### Implementation Arrangements

The implementation arrangements will be as follows:

- The project will be implemented by the Government of Guatemala, with technical assistance from FAO and FAD.
- The project will be implemented over a period of five years.
- The project will be implemented in the Dry Corridor of Guatemala.

### Monitoring and Evaluation

The monitoring and evaluation arrangements will be as follows:

- The project will be monitored by an independent consultant.
- The project will be evaluated at the end of each year.
- The project will be subject to a final evaluation at the end of the implementation period.

### Financial Arrangements

The financial arrangements will be as follows:

- The project will be financed by FAO and FAD.
- The project will be implemented in accordance with the financial regulations of Guatemala.
- The project will be subject to annual audits by the Government of Guatemala.

### Risk Management

The risk management arrangements will be as follows:

- The project will be subject to a risk assessment at the outset.
- The project will be subject to regular risk reviews throughout the implementation period.
- The project will be subject to a final risk review at the end of the implementation period.

### Environmental and Social Safeguards

The environmental and social safeguards arrangements will be as follows:

- The project will be subject to an environmental impact assessment.
- The project will be subject to a social impact assessment.
- The project will be subject to regular monitoring of environmental and social impacts throughout the implementation period.

### Gender Equality and Social Inclusion

The gender equality and social inclusion arrangements will be as follows:

- The project will be designed to promote gender equality and social inclusion.
- The project will be subject to a gender impact assessment.
- The project will be subject to regular monitoring of gender equality and social inclusion throughout the implementation period.

### Communication and Information Management

The communication and information management arrangements will be as follows:

- The project will have a communication strategy.
- The project will have a website for information dissemination.
- The project will have regular updates on progress through newsletters and reports.

### Coordination with Other Actors

The coordination with other actors arrangements will be as follows:

- The project will coordinate with other actors in the Dry Corridor.
- The project will participate in regional and national forums to promote ESA.
- The project will share lessons learned with other projects and programs.

66. **The Risk Based Lending (RBL) credit line will be established:** to complement the GCF's Quick Start Facility and CABI (US$42.5 million) which is designed to support the implementation of projects in developing countries. The RBL credit line will provide financing for projects that are considered high risk but have a high potential impact on climate change mitigation and adaptation. This includes projects that are not eligible for traditional financing, such as those that involve innovative technologies or require long-term financing.

# Financing Scheme - Lending and Guarantee facilities

## Loan through the Global Credit Line (GCL)

### GCL

#### Component Facilities

- **Component Facility 1**
  - Description: Provides financing for climate change mitigation and adaptation projects.
  - Eligibility: Available to IBRD/IFC borrowers with a credit rating of A or better.

- **Component Facility 2**
  - Description: Supports projects that reduce greenhouse gas emissions and enhance carbon sequestration.
  - Eligibility: Available to IDA borrowers with a credit rating of B or better.

#### Financing Terms

- **Interest Rate**: Based on the Global Credit Line (GCL) interest rate.
- **Tenor**: Up to 40 years, including a grace period of up to 10 years.
- **Covenants**: Standard covenants apply.

### Figure 17: Lending and Guarantee Facility Financing Scheme

68. By promoting a blended financing mechanism composed of concessional finance from GCP in combination with additional resources from the public sector, private sector, or other sources, the GCL seeks to leverage more commercial finance for climate change mitigation and adaptation projects.

69. The GCL lending facility is designed to support projects that address climate change issues such as deforestation, water scarcity, and energy efficiency. It also aims to promote the use of renewable energy technologies like solar, wind, hydro, and geothermal power.

70. Financial institutions will be able to access the GCL lending facility by obtaining a loan from their respective central bank or commercial bank. The loan amount will depend on the institution's creditworthiness and the project's feasibility.

71. Highly credible climate change mitigation projects are eligible for financing under the GCL lending facility. These projects must meet certain criteria, such as reducing greenhouse gas emissions by at least 20% compared to a baseline scenario.

## III. Enhancing Capacity and a Technology Option for PIFs to Access the EA Funding Facility

The EA Secretariat will provide technical assistance to PIFs to access the EA funding facility. This process will be facilitated through the development of a technology option for PIFs, which will include the following steps:

1. **Identify Needs**: Assess the needs and priorities of PIFs.
2. **Develop Options**: Develop technology options that meet these needs.
3. **Provide Assistance**: Provide technical assistance to implement these options.

### 76. Enhancing Capacity

The EA Secretariat will enhance capacity by providing technical assistance and a technology option for PIFs to access the EA funding facility. This process will involve:

- Identifying needs and priorities of PIFs.
- Developing technology options that meet these needs.
- Providing technical assistance to implement these options.

### 77. The T&F Facility

The T&F facility aims to provide technical assistance and a comprehensive training program for PIFs. This will include:

- **Training Programs**: Development of training programs tailored to the needs of PIFs.
- **Producing and Distributing Materials**: Production and distribution of materials that support these training programs.

### 78. The Implementation Plan

The implementation plan will be developed in collaboration with relevant stakeholders, including:

- **Consultation with Stakeholders**: Consultation with stakeholders to ensure the plan meets their needs.
- **Development of a Timeline**: Development of a timeline for implementation.

### Launching the T&F Facility

The T&F facility will be launched through the EA Secretariat and will include the following steps:

1. **Identify Needs**: Identify the needs of PIFs.
2. **Develop Options**: Develop technology options that meet these needs.
3. **Provide Assistance**: Provide technical assistance to implement these options.

### Restructuring the T&F Facility

As a result of the initial phase, the technical team (including PIFs) will conduct an information campaign about the EA funding facility and the technology option for PIFs. This will include:

- **Information Campaign**: Conducting an information campaign to raise awareness.
- **Workshops and Training Sessions**: Organizing workshops and training sessions.

### Scaling Up the T&F Facility

The technical team will also conduct a needs assessment of the EA Secretariat and undertake sustainable development activities. During this phase, the following steps will be taken:

1. **Needs Assessment**: Conducting a needs assessment.
2. **Development Activities**: Undertaking sustainable development activities.

### Detailed Analysis for the T&F Facility in the Dry Corridor

The technical team will also conduct detailed analysis for the T&F facility in the dry corridor, including:

- **Detailed Analysis**: Conducting detailed analysis to inform the development of the technology option.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2 | PAGE 4 OF 8

## Executive Summary

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a financial mechanism under the Convention to promote a paradigm shift towards low-emission and climate-resilient development pathways.

### Key Results Areas

- **Accreditation of Entities**: 13.73
  - Accredited entities include both implementing entities and accredited entities.
  - Entities are accredited through a rigorous process that includes an initial screening, due diligence, and ongoing monitoring to ensure compliance with GCF standards.

- **Approved Projects**: 8.45
  - The number of projects approved by the GCF Board.
  - Projects span various sectors including renewable energy, climate-smart agriculture, and disaster risk reduction.

- **Disbursed Resources**: 6.32
  - Total resources disbursed to support projects and programs.
  - Disbursements are made in accordance with approved funding proposals and project budgets.

### Implementation

The Green Climate Fund aims for full implementation of the grant-financed activities, co-financing arrangements, and other financial instruments. The GCF will continue to work closely with its accredited entities to ensure effective implementation of projects and programs.

#### Key Objectives

- **Enhance Access**: Increase access to climate finance for developing countries.
  - Focus on supporting vulnerable communities and small island developing states (SIDS).
  - Promote gender-responsive climate action.

- **Strengthen Capacity**: Build capacity in developing countries to effectively manage GCF resources.
  - Provide technical assistance and training programs.
  - Foster partnerships with regional and international organizations.

- **Promote Transparency**: Ensure transparency and accountability in the use of GCF funds.
  - Implement robust monitoring and evaluation systems.
  - Publish annual reports on project progress and financial statements.

### Stakeholder Engagement

The Green Climate Fund will also focus on full implementation at the national level and results from investments will serve as input for national climate change strategies. These results will also be shared with stakeholders to enhance transparency and accountability.

#### Free Implementation Plan

The free implementation plan of the GCF is described below:

- **Locating the Funding**: Identify sources of funding.
  - Government grants
  - Private sector investments
  - International aid

- **Preparing and Submitting a Proposal**: Develop and submit a proposal to the GCF.
  - Project design
  - Budget planning
  - Risk assessment

- **Project Implementation**: Execute the project according to the approved plan.
  - Resource mobilization
  - Capacity building
  - Monitoring and evaluation

### Additional Information

For more information on the implementation process, please refer to the [Green Climate Fund website](https://www.greenclimate.fund).

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2 | PAGE 4 OF 8

## Introduction to the Proposal

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a new and innovative financing mechanism that aims to support climate change mitigation and adaptation projects in developing countries. The GCF was established under the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) and has been operational since 2015.

### Objectives of the GCF

The primary objectives of the GCF are:

- To promote a paradigm shift towards low-emission and climate-resilient development pathways by providing support to developing countries.
- To catalyze and scale up investment in projects, policies, and technologies that address the causes and effects of climate change.

### Funding Mechanism

The GCF operates through two funding windows: the Mitigation Window and the Adaptation Window. These windows provide financial resources to developing countries for climate change mitigation and adaptation activities respectively.

#### Mitigation Window

The Mitigation Window aims to support projects and programs that reduce greenhouse gas emissions in developing countries. This includes initiatives such as renewable energy projects, energy efficiency improvements, and sustainable transport systems.

#### Adaptation Window

The Adaptation Window focuses on supporting projects and programs that enhance the resilience of developing countries to the adverse effects of climate change. This includes initiatives such as water management, early warning systems, and climate-smart agriculture.

### Eligibility Criteria

To be eligible for GCF funding, a project or program must:

- Be aligned with the objectives of the Paris Agreement.
- Contribute to the achievement of nationally determined contributions (NDCs) submitted by developing countries.
- Be implemented in accordance with GCF policies and procedures.

## Implementation Strategy

The implementation strategy for the GCF involves several key components:

1. **Accreditation of Entities**: The GCF accredits entities that are capable of managing funds effectively and efficiently. These entities include national designated authorities, regional organizations, and private sector entities.
2. **Project Preparation Facility (PPF)**: The PPF provides financial support to developing countries for the preparation and development of climate change mitigation and adaptation projects.
3. **Direct Access Entities**: Direct access entities are national designated authorities or accredited entities that can directly access GCF funds without the need for an implementing entity.

### Key Milestones

- **2015**: The GCF became operational with the approval of its initial resources.
- **2016**: The first set of projects were approved and funding disbursed.
- **2017**: The GCF Board adopted the Private Sector Facility to enhance private sector engagement in climate change mitigation and adaptation.

### Financial Resources

The GCF has pledged USD 10.3 billion for its initial resource mobilization period (2015-2020). This funding comes from various sources, including contributions from developed countries, private sector investments, and other international financial institutions.

## Conclusion

The Green Climate Fund represents a significant step forward in global efforts to address climate change. By providing financial support to developing countries for both mitigation and adaptation activities, the GCF plays a crucial role in achieving the goals of the Paris Agreement. The successful implementation of GCF-funded projects will require continued collaboration between developed and developing countries, as well as effective engagement with private sector entities.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.2 | PAGE 1 OF N

## EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a multilateral fund established to support developing countries and vulnerable communities in their efforts to address the causes and effects of climate change. The GCF was created as one of the key mechanisms for financing activities under the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). It aims to promote a paradigm shift towards low-emission and climate-resilient development pathways.

### The GCF

The GCF is the operational arm of the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) and serves as the largest dedicated fund for climate change mitigation and adaptation. It was established in 2010 to support developing countries in their efforts to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and adapt to climate change. The GCF is governed by a Board composed of equal numbers of representatives from developed and developing countries, with decisions made on an equal basis.

### Accreditation Process

The accreditation process for entities seeking to access GCF funds involves several steps:

1. **Initial Screening**: Entities must pass an initial screening to ensure they meet the basic requirements.
2. **Due Diligence**: A thorough due diligence process is conducted to assess the entity's capacity and commitment to climate change activities.
3. **Board Approval**: The final decision on accreditation is made by the GCF Board.

### Key Objectives

The GCF aims to:

- Support projects that contribute to both mitigation and adaptation efforts.
- Promote a paradigm shift towards low-emission and climate-resilient development pathways.
- Enhance the resilience of developing countries and communities to the adverse effects of climate change.

## Introduction

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a multilateral fund established to support developing countries in their efforts to address the causes and effects of climate change. The GCF was created as one of the key mechanisms for financing activities under the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). It aims to promote a paradigm shift towards low-emission and climate-resilient development pathways.

### The GCF

The GCF is the operational arm of the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) and serves as the largest dedicated fund for climate change mitigation and adaptation. It was established in 2010 to support developing countries in their efforts to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and adapt to climate change. The GCF is governed by a Board composed of equal numbers of representatives from developed and developing countries, with decisions made on an equal basis.

### Accreditation Process

The accreditation process for entities seeking to access GCF funds involves several steps:

1. **Initial Screening**: Entities must pass an initial screening to ensure they meet the basic requirements.
2. **Due Diligence**: A thorough due diligence process is conducted to assess the entity's capacity and commitment to climate change activities.
3. **Board Approval**: The final decision on accreditation is made by the GCF Board.

### Key Objectives

The GCF aims to:

## The FAO

The Food and Agriculture Organization (FAO) is an organization with the goal of reducing food insecurity and malnutrition, enhancing agricultural productivity, improving the lives of rural populations, and protecting natural resources. The FAO supports projects that contribute to sustainable agriculture and rural development.

### Key Objectives

- **Food Security**: Ensuring access to safe and nutritious food.
- **Rural Development**: Improving the livelihoods of rural communities.
- **Natural Resource Management**: Protecting and sustainably managing natural resources.

## The Proposal

This proposal outlines a request for funding from the GCF to support projects that contribute to sustainable agriculture and rural development. The FAO, in collaboration with the GCF, aims to implement projects that enhance food security, improve livelihoods, and protect natural resources.

### Key Components of the Proposal

- **GCF/FAO Result (VERM/RES/01)**: A comprehensive overview of the proposed activities.
- **Implementation Strategy**: Detailed plans for project implementation.
- **Monitoring and Evaluation**: Mechanisms to track progress and assess impact.
- **Financial Management**: Plans for financial management and reporting.

## Conclusion

The GCF and FAO collaboration represents a significant step towards addressing climate change and promoting sustainable development. This proposal outlines the key components of the proposed activities, including implementation strategies, monitoring and evaluation mechanisms, and financial management plans. The successful implementation of these projects will contribute to enhanced food security, improved livelihoods, and protected natural resources.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

## Objective

The objective is to leverage additional agreement funds from the Green Climate Fund (GCF) to support the implementation of the FAO project "Climate Smart Agriculture Systems for the Dry Corridor of Central America which support the resilience and adaptation of smallholder farmers to climate change impacts."

### 1.2. Project Components
- The proposed technical assistance project (US$4.5 million) and "Strengthening the resilience of multiple value chains in the dry corridor" project (US$30.8 million).
- Promote climate-smart agriculture management project under the Dominican Republic (DR) (US$17.9 million).
- The FAO project will work with local governments, project (US$5.1 million), and the Adaptation to Climate Change project (ACCC) (US$30.8 million).
- The FAO project will support the implementation of the FAO project "Climate Smart Agriculture Systems for the Dry Corridor of Central America which support the resilience and adaptation of smallholder farmers to climate change impacts."

### 1.2.1 Project Rationale
The FAO project aims to strengthen the resilience of smallholder farmers in the dry corridor by promoting climate-smart agriculture practices that increase productivity, improve food security, and enhance the adaptive capacity of vulnerable households.

### 1.3. Project Implementation Arrangements
- The GCF project will be implemented through a series of activities for the implementation of the FAO project "Climate Smart Agriculture Systems for the Dry Corridor of Central America which support the resilience and adaptation of smallholder farmers to climate change impacts."
- The GCF project will be implemented by the FAO in collaboration with local governments, project (US$5.1 million), and the Adaptation to Climate Change project (ACCC) (US$30.8 million).

### 1.4. Project Management
The GCF project will be managed by the FAO project "Climate Smart Agriculture Systems for the Dry Corridor of Central America which support the resilience and adaptation of smallholder farmers to climate change impacts."

### 1.5. Project Funding
UN Environment will play a technical advisory role for the program through their participation in the GCF project "Climate Smart Agriculture Systems for the Dry Corridor of Central America which support the resilience and adaptation of smallholder farmers to climate change impacts."

### 1.6. Project Results Framework
The GCF project aims to strengthen the resilience of smallholder farmers by promoting climate-smart agriculture practices that increase productivity, improve food security, and enhance the adaptive capacity of vulnerable households.

### 1.7. Project Implementation Arrangements
- The GCF project will be implemented through a series of activities for the implementation of the FAO project "Climate Smart Agriculture Systems for the Dry Corridor of Central America which support the resilience and adaptation of smallholder farmers to climate change impacts."
- The GCF project will be implemented by the FAO in collaboration with local governments, project (US$5.1 million), and the Adaptation to Climate Change project (ACCC) (US$30.8 million).

### 1.8. Project Management
The GCF project will be managed by the FAO project "Climate Smart Agriculture Systems for the Dry Corridor of Central America which support the resilience and adaptation of smallholder farmers to climate change impacts."

### 1.9. Project Funding
UN Environment will play a technical advisory role for the program through their participation in the GCF project "Climate Smart Agriculture Systems for the Dry Corridor of Central America which support the resilience and adaptation of smallholder farmers to climate change impacts."

### RFP Management Committee

The committee will oversee the entire process of the RFP, including the preparation of the RFP document, the evaluation of proposals, and the selection of the winning proposal.

### RFP Process Management

The RFP process management will be carried out by FAO's regional representatives in Panama and will be responsible for day-to-day coordination. The RFP Manager will ensure that all activities are conducted according to the timeline established by the RFP Management Committee. The RFP Manager will also be responsible for ensuring that the RFP is compliant with GCF requirements.

## Project Execution Units

Project Execution Units (PEUs) will be established in the largest sub-national of each of the participating countries. Each PEU will be composed of a team of local experts and an Executive Unit Coordinator who will be responsible for the overall management of the project. The PEUs will be responsible for the implementation, monitoring, evaluation, and reporting on the activities carried out under the GCF. They will ensure that the projects are implemented in accordance with the approved work plans and budgets, and that they comply with the relevant policies and procedures established by the GCF.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2 | PAGE 1 OF 8

## Summary and Needs in recent years (Table 1)

In recent years, a significant number of people have been affected by natural disasters such as hurricanes, floods, and droughts. The impact of these events has been devastating. After Hurricane Irma in 2017, 95% of the population was affected. In 2018, 1.6 million people were affected by Hurricane Dorian. The COVID-19 pandemic has exacerbated the situation, with an estimated 30% to 40% increase in unemployment and poverty rates.

### Co-benefits of Green Climate Fund (GCF) projects

The GCF is a valuable resource for countries that are vulnerable to natural disasters. It provides funding for projects that aim to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and adapt to the impacts of climate change. The GCF can help countries build resilience, improve public health, and increase access to clean energy.

### Table 1: Impact of natural disasters on the population (2008-2019)

| Country/Region | Affected People |
|----------------|-----------------|
| Cuba            | 715,000         |
| Dominican Republic | 345,000       |
| Haiti           | 610,000         |
| Guatemala       | 289,000         |
| Honduras        | 110,000         |
| Nicaragua       | 75,000          |
| Panama          | 345,000         |
| Puerto Rico     | 610,000         |

### COVID-19 pandemic

The COVID-19 pandemic is causing additional negative impacts on healthcare systems and economies. The pandemic has exacerbated existing inequalities and increased the vulnerability of marginalized communities to climate-related events. This includes low-income households and frontline workers who are more likely to be exposed to health risks while dealing with triple threats to food, water, and shelter.

### To mitigate current and predicted climate impacts in the Dry Corridor and Aid Zones

It is necessary to implement adaptation measures that reduce vulnerability and increase resilience. These include improving access to clean energy, promoting sustainable agriculture, and enhancing early warning systems. Currently, there are limited resources available for these initiatives. The GCF can provide funding to support these efforts.

### Table 2: COVID-19 cases by country, not including deaths, as of October 2020

| Country/Region | Cases |
|----------------|-------|
| Cuba            | 7,500  |
| Dominican Republic | 3,450 |
| Haiti           | 6,100  |
| Guatemala       | 289,000 |
| Honduras        | 110,000 |
| Nicaragua       | 75,000  |
| Panama          | 345,000 |
| Puerto Rico     | 6,100   |

### References

- United Nations Office for Disaster Risk Reduction (UNISDR). Regional Office for Latin America and the Caribbean. 2019. Disasters in Latin America and the Caribbean: A Review of the Last Decade.
- World Bank. 2018. Climate-Smart Development: Adding Up the Benefits of Actions that Help Build Prosperity, End Extreme Poverty, and Fight Climate Change.

## Additional Information

Although countries in the region are considered frontline states, they have been marginalized from international climate finance due to their low capacity and lack of access to information. This proposal aims to address this gap by providing a comprehensive approach that includes:

- Enhancing national adaptation planning processes.
- Improving access to climate finance.
- Strengthening institutional capacities for effective implementation.

## Africa LCAT Fund

Africa LCAT fund is a highly specialized non-profit organization established to provide technical assistance and capacity building support to countries in the region. The fund aims to address the challenges faced by developing countries in accessing international climate finance. The fund will focus on providing:

- Technical assistance.
- Capacity building.
- Access to information.

The fund will be managed by a team of experts with extensive experience in climate change mitigation and adaptation. The fund will also work closely with other international organizations to ensure effective implementation of projects.

## Part I

### 14.3. Total annual contributions
The total annual contributions to the GCF from public and private sources are expected to be around USD 10 billion by 2025. This is based on the current trend of increasing contributions from both developed and developing countries. The GCF aims to mobilize additional resources from the private sector and other sources to achieve this target.

### 14.3.1. Contribution levels
The contribution levels are expected to increase significantly over the next few years, with a target of USD 2 billion per year by 2025. This is in line with the GCF's goal of mobilizing additional resources from both public and private sources.

### 14.3.2. Contribution mechanisms
The contribution mechanisms will include both mandatory and voluntary contributions from developed countries, as well as additional resources from developing countries. The GCF will also explore innovative financing mechanisms to mobilize additional resources.

The funding priorities for the GCF include support for climate change mitigation and adaptation projects, as well as capacity building and technology transfer initiatives. The GCF will also prioritize support for vulnerable countries and communities.

The funding mechanisms for the GCF will include grants, concessional loans, and equity investments. The GCF will also explore innovative financing mechanisms to mobilize additional resources.

The ex ante strategy and sustainability of the proposed programme are crucial for ensuring that the GCF is able to achieve its objectives in a sustainable manner.

The GCF will also develop a monitoring and evaluation framework to track progress towards its objectives.

The post-programme evaluation and monitoring mechanisms for the proposed programme are designed to ensure that the GCF is able to achieve its objectives in a sustainable manner.

## GEF Green Climate Fund Proposal

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a financial mechanism established to support activities for mitigation and adaptation to climate change in developing countries, particularly those that are vulnerable to the adverse effects of climate change. The GCF was established as an operating entity of the Financial Mechanism of the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). The GCF supports projects, programmes, policies, and other activities in accordance with the principles and provisions of the UNFCCC.

### 115. Monitoring and evaluation

Lessons learned from programme activities will be collated in an M&E Special Report for the Green Climate Fund Management Unit and made available to development partners through a web-based platform. The lessons learned will include information on the effectiveness of the GCF's support for climate change mitigation and adaptation, including the impact of the GCF's support on the achievement of the GCF's strategic objectives.

#### F. Eligibility criteria

The eligibility criteria for the GCF financing instrument are as follows:

- The project must be in accordance with the GCF's policies and procedures.
- The project must contribute to the achievement of the GCF's strategic objectives.
- The project must be implemented in a developing country that is a party to the UNFCCC.

#### G. Application process

The application process for the GCF financing instrument involves the following steps:

1. Preparation of an initial concept note.
2. Submission of the concept note to the GCF Secretariat.
3. Review and evaluation of the concept note by the GCF Secretariat.
4. Approval of the concept note by the GCF Board.
5. Preparation of a detailed project proposal.
6. Submission of the project proposal to the GCF Secretariat.
7. Review and evaluation of the project proposal by the GCF Secretariat.
8. Approval of the project proposal by the GCF Board.
9. Disbursement of funds.

#### H. Reporting requirements

The reporting requirements for the GCF financing instrument include:

- Annual progress reports.
- Semi-annual financial reports.
- Final evaluation report.

152. Capacity building will be provided through several programme activities. Activity 6.3 will provide technical assistance (TA) to strengthen the technical capacity of ECOWAS as well as non-state actors to implement climate change adaptation and mitigation projects. The TA will also support the development of a regional climate change information system for ECOWAS member states. This activity will be implemented by the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP) under activity 6.3, led by UNDP.

153. Policy and regulatory frameworks are needed to create enabling environments, including the review of existing policies, the development of the Dry Corridor and Agro-Zones policies, and the revision of the National Adaptation Plan (NAP). This will be implemented through activity 6.4, led by UNDP.

154. Technology transfer will be conducted through Activity 3.1 when a regional knowledge management hub is established to facilitate the exchange of best practices among member states. This will also be supported by a regional climate change information system (105.8/27.6).

---

### EXPECTED RESULTS

169. The proposed programme will contribute to the achievement of GCF's long-term adaptation objectives and the ECOWAS Regional Climate Change Policy (RCCCP) through the following expected results:

- **Expected Result 1:** The Dry Corridor and Agro-Zones policies are developed and implemented.
- **Expected Result 2:** The National Adaptation Plan is revised to align with the RCCCP.
- **Expected Result 3:** A regional knowledge management hub is established to facilitate the exchange of best practices among member states.

## Adaptation Benefits

### 105

If adapted to the selected impacts, the proposed project could contribute to three broad-based outcomes. The project would reduce risks and enhance resilience. Additionally, it would lower the project's vulnerability to climate change.

### 106

**ASI** — Strengthened institutional and regulatory systems for climate-resilient planning and management of natural resources will be achieved through improved governance and enhanced capacity to manage EIA and other environment-related procedures in local development plans and policies. This will contribute to the achievement of sustainable development goals (SDGs) and support climate change adaptation.

### 107

**AFI** — Strengthened adaptive capacity and reduced exposure to climate risks. The adoption of EIA processes, improved governance, and enhanced capacity for climate-resilient planning and management will reduce vulnerability to climate impacts. Improved governance will also contribute to the achievement of SDGs. The project will support the development of a National Adaptation Plan (NAP) that will be aligned with the Nationally Determined Contribution (NDC). This will enhance the country's capacity to adapt to climate change.

### 108

**ASI** — Strengthened awareness of climate risks and risk reduction processes. The proposed project will contribute to improved public understanding of climate risks, which is a key component of sustainable communities. It will also facilitate the programme as a foundation for the effectiveness of other projects that aim to build resilient and local communities.

## D.1. Participating small proposal

### 106
The proposed programme will utilise a phased unit in the initial approach to establish and strengthen the capacity of EEA and water-efficient technologies. The programme will be implemented through the following key activities:

- **Purpose:** The goal is to see generating promising high-value EEA, and the proper order including the use of water-efficient technologies.
- **Objectives:** To enhance the technical skills required by different stakeholder adaptation initiatives.

### 107
The proposed EEA programme is designed to be flexible and only within the targeted beneficiaries but also to know the entire EEA system and EIA. The programme will be carefully designed to reach an effective approach to the implementation of the programme. The goal of these three areas is to support the development of water-efficient technologies.

### Creation of an enabling environment for investment in EEA and water-efficient technologies

#### 108
The proposed programme will create an enabling environment for EEA and water-efficient technologies. This will include:

- **Purpose:** To establish a framework that supports the implementation of EEA and water-efficient technologies.
- **Objectives:** To support the development of policies, regulations, and standards to facilitate investment in EEA and water-efficient technologies.

### Consolidation of the regulatory framework and policies

#### 109
The proposed programme will consolidate the regulatory framework for EEA and water-efficient technologies. This will include:

- **Purpose:** To create a comprehensive regulatory framework that supports the implementation of EEA and water-efficient technologies.
- **Objectives:** To support the development of policies, regulations, and standards to facilitate investment in EEA and water-efficient technologies.

### Technical support for the enhancement of regional knowledge and training

#### 110
The proposed programme will provide technical support for the enhancement of regional knowledge and training. This will include:

- **Purpose:** To enhance the capacity of stakeholders to implement EEA and water-efficient technologies.
- **Objectives:** To support the development of policies, regulations, and standards to facilitate investment in EEA and water-efficient technologies.

### Up-scaling of adoption

#### 111
The proposed programme will up-scale the autonomous adoption of EEA interventions and water-efficient technologies. This will include:

- **Purpose:** To promote the widespread adoption of EEA interventions and water-efficient technologies.
- **Objectives:** To support the development of policies, regulations, and standards to facilitate investment in EEA and water-efficient technologies.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2 | PAGE 1 OF 8

## Introduction

Green Climate Fund (GCF) Readiness and Preparatory Support (RPS) proposals should be designed to address the needs of the country in which they are implemented, taking into account the country's circumstances and priorities. The RPS programme is designed to support developing countries to prepare for GCF funding by providing resources to build their capacity and systems for effective climate change mitigation and adaptation.

## Programme Objectives

The proposed programme will contribute to the achievement of one or more of the following objectives:

- **Objective 1: Strengthen National Designated Authorities (NDAs) and Accredited Entities (AEs)**
  - The main contributions of the programme will be to:
    - NDA: Enhance the capacity of NDAs to effectively manage GCF resources.
    - AE: Support AEs in accessing GCF funding.

- **Objective 2: Develop and Implement National Strategies**
  - The programme aims to support countries in developing and implementing national strategies for climate change mitigation and adaptation.

## Programme Components

The programme will include the following components:

1. **Component 1: Institutional Strengthening**
   - Support NDAs and AEs in strengthening their institutional capacity.
   - Enhance the capacity of national designated authorities (NDAs) to effectively manage GCF resources.

2. **Component 2: Technical Assistance**
   - Provide technical assistance to support countries in developing and implementing national strategies for climate change mitigation and adaptation.

3. **Component 3: Capacity Building**
   - Build the capacity of NDAs, AEs, and other relevant stakeholders.
   - Support the development of human resources and institutional capacities required for effective implementation of GCF-funded projects.

## Programme Implementation

The programme will be implemented over a period of five years, with an indicative budget of USD 10 million. The programme will be managed by the GCF Secretariat, which will provide technical assistance and oversight to ensure that the objectives are achieved.

## Expected Outcomes

- **Outcome 1: Strengthened NDAs**
  - NDAs have enhanced capacity to manage GCF resources effectively.
  - AEs have improved access to GCF funding.

- **Outcome 2: National Strategies Developed and Implemented**
  - Countries have developed and are implementing national strategies for climate change mitigation and adaptation.

## Programme Budget

The indicative budget for the programme is USD 10 million, which will be used to support the implementation of the programme over a period of five years. The budget will be allocated across the different components of the programme to ensure effective implementation.

## Programme Management

The programme will be managed by the GCF Secretariat, which will provide technical assistance and oversight to ensure that the objectives are achieved. The programme will be implemented in close collaboration with relevant stakeholders, including national governments, civil society organizations, and other relevant actors.
