# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2 | PAGE 8 OF 9

## Social co-benefits

The proposed programme is expected to provide substantial social co-benefits, mostly contributing to:

- **Local economic development**
    - Job creation through small and medium enterprises (SMEs) with sustainable harvesting systems.
    - Increased access to clean water for domestic use as well as food production.
    - Improved livelihoods through increased agricultural productivity.
    - Enhanced food security and nutrition.

- **Gender equality**
    - The programme will empower women by providing them with equal opportunities in the management of natural resources.
    - Women's participation in decision-making processes will be enhanced through capacity building, leadership training, and the provision of microfinance services to support their economic empowerment.

## Externality co-benefits

- Access to water and the provision of EIA will have several ancillary benefits for sustainable development in the Central Amazon by promoting and the Deforestation-Free Approach. These include:
    - The implementation of water-efficient technologies – such as drip irrigation and rainwater harvesting – is vital.
    - Improved access to water will reduce the pressure on forests, thereby reducing deforestation and forest degradation.
    - Enhanced food security through improved agricultural productivity.

- **Climate change mitigation**
    - The programme will contribute to climate change mitigation by promoting sustainable agriculture practices that enhance carbon sequestration in soils.
    - Reduced emissions from deforestation and forest degradation (REDD+) activities will be supported, contributing to global efforts to mitigate climate change.

## Gender-sensitive environmental impact

(7) The programme will ensure gender equality and women's empowerment through:

- **Capacity building**
    - Training on sustainable harvesting techniques for women.
    - Provision of microfinance services to support women's entrepreneurship.
    - Promotion of women's leadership in natural resource management.

- **Access to resources**
    - Ensuring equal access to water, land, and other productive resources for women.
    - Support for women's participation in decision-making processes related to natural resource management.

## Contribution to recovery post COVID-19

The programme will contribute to the economic recovery of communities affected by COVID-19 through:

- **Job creation**
    - Employment opportunities in sustainable agriculture and forestry sectors.
    - Support for small and medium enterprises (SMEs) in the green economy.

- **Improved livelihoods**
    - Enhanced food security and nutrition through improved agricultural productivity.
    - Increased access to clean water, reducing the burden on women and girls who traditionally bear the responsibility of water collection.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.2 | PAGE 1 OF N

## The COVID-19 pandemic is having severe impacts on households systems and communities around the world.

The pandemic has exacerbated existing inequalities and vulnerabilities, with women, children, youth, elderly, people with disabilities, and marginalized groups disproportionately affected. Climate change exacerbates these impacts by increasing extreme weather events that cause food insecurity and displacement. This is a critical solution in addressing climate change and ensuring sustainable development. The COVID-19 pandemic has highlighted the need for universal health care and social protection systems to ensure that all people have access to quality health services, regardless of their ability to pay.

### Vulnerability

The Dry Corridor region of Central America suffers from chronic food insecurity and poverty. The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is active in the region through its support for projects that address climate change impacts on agriculture and food security. GCF-funded projects aim to improve the livelihoods of smallholder farmers, particularly women and youth, by providing them with access to new technologies and markets.

The Dry Corridor region of Central America suffers from chronic food insecurity and poverty. The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is active in the region through its support for projects that address climate change impacts on agriculture and food security. GCF-funded projects aim to improve the livelihoods of smallholder farmers, particularly women and youth, by providing them with access to new technologies and markets.

### Recommendations

- Increase investment in health care systems.
- Strengthen social protection programs.
- Promote gender equality and women's empowerment.
- Support climate change adaptation and mitigation efforts.

Based on the latest IPCC report, it is clear that the Latin America and Caribbean (LAC) region — under which the seven beneficiary countries fall — is highly vulnerable to climate change. The region is already experiencing an increase in the frequency of extreme weather events such as hurricanes, droughts, and floods. This has led to an overall reduction in primary food production and expansion of the middle class. Currently, the lower beneficiary countries are facing a severe economic crisis that has been exacerbated by the COVID-19 pandemic.

## Need for a strengthened commitment and implementation capacity

### 170

As the global lead, developed countries do not bear the financial resources; knowledge base; technical expertise; and human capital to address climate change alone. They need to build their capacities in developing countries, including through South-South cooperation.

- The need for a clear understanding of the context in which the project is proposed.
- The need to ensure that the project is consistent with the objectives and principles of the GCF.

- The Medical Agro-environmental and Health Strategy of Central America (2019-2024) aims to promote sustainable development in the region, ensuring the conservation of natural resources, biodiversity, and ecosystem services.
- It seeks to reduce environmental risks, improve health conditions, and strengthen the resilience of populations exposed to climate change. The strategy also aims to develop appropriate food and nutrition systems.
- The strategy includes actions to achieve sustainable health and cultural approaches that contribute to the development of resilient communities, capable of adapting to climate change, reducing vulnerabilities, and the impact of disasters on human health.
- The strategy considers the need to build the capacities of local governments to implement the strategy and to promote the integration of climate change adaptation measures in national policies.

The Strategy and Plan for Integrated Water Resource Management (IWRM) is part of Central America's (2019-2024) Regional Agenda, which aims to ensure the efficient and sustainable management of water resources.

In addition to the regional agenda, this program contributes to achieving national priorities and objectives related to climate change adaptation and disaster risk reduction, as well as the E. Feeding Strategy.

## Capacity of the Accredited and Designated Entities to deliver

### 165.
The Canadian American Border for Economic Integration (CAMEI) will be the Accredited Entity (AE) for this proposal. CAMEI is a non-profit organization that has been working with the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) since its inception in 2009. CAMEI has extensive experience in delivering climate change mitigation and adaptation projects funded by GCF, such as those implemented through the Enabling Conditions program.

### 165.
Though a network of interrelated financial and non-financial institutions, CAMEI has achieved a strong reputation for its ability to deliver high-quality programs. CAMEI has developed a robust governance structure that ensures transparency, accountability, and effective management of funds. The AE is well-positioned to support the delivery of this proposal.

### 165.
The program will be co-implemented by three entities: CAMEI, FAO, and CCAD. FAO has been working with GCF since its inception in 2014. FAO is a specialized agency of the United Nations that leads international efforts to defeat hunger. FAO supports countries in developing policies, programs, and projects that help achieve food security worldwide. FAO has worked with CAMEI on several occasions.

### 165.
The CCAD is an intergovernmental organization that works closely with FAO and other UN agencies to support the implementation of the Paris Agreement. The CCAD is a key partner in the delivery of this proposal, providing expertise and resources for project development and implementation.

### 165.
The development of this program was informed by the Council of Ministers of the CCAD, which includes ministers from all the countries that are part of the CCAD region. This process included consultations with national governments, civil society organizations, and other stakeholders to ensure that the program is aligned with regional priorities.

### 165.
Further consultations took place at the International Conference on Agricultural Research for Development (ICARDA) in October 2017. ICARDA is a global research organization that works to reduce poverty, enhance food and nutrition security, and improve natural resource management from dryland systems in the region.

### 165.
The program will be implemented by CAMEI, FAO, and CCAD. The implementation period for this proposal is five years, starting from January 2023 to December 2027. This timeline aligns with the GCF funding cycle and ensures that the project can achieve its objectives within a reasonable timeframe.

### 165.
The program will be implemented in accordance with the GCF's policies and procedures, including those related to environmental and social safeguards, gender equality, and climate change adaptation. The program will also comply with all relevant laws and regulations of the countries where the projects are implemented.

### 165.
The program will be monitored and evaluated by an independent third-party entity to ensure that it is achieving its objectives and delivering results in a transparent and accountable manner. The monitoring and evaluation framework will include indicators for tracking progress towards the program's goals and outcomes.

### 165.
The program will also comply with all relevant laws and regulations of the countries where the projects are implemented. This includes obtaining any necessary permits or approvals, as well as complying with environmental and social safeguards requirements.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2 | PAGE 8 OF 8

GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL

The proposal outlines the evaluation of the Gorongosa Nave and the development of the Funding Proposal. First, it describes the process of evaluating the project's feasibility through a series of consultations with local communities to get community input on the project. Second, it describes the process of developing a funding strategy that includes identifying potential sources of funding and developing a budget.

## As for Financial technical assistance

The CARRI team works closely with information from a financial structure consultant to develop a detailed financial plan. The plan will include an assessment of the project's financial needs and an analysis of the project's financial feasibility. Further, the plan will also include recommendations on how to improve the project's financial sustainability.

### DSL: Emissions and efficiencies

The proposal takes interventions on multi-stakeholder levels. It aims to prepare for EBA (Emission Based Allocation) and to develop a strategy for the implementation of the project. The project is expected to be implemented in 2023 with an estimated budget of $1,500,000. The project will focus on developing local capacity and promoting sustainable practices. It aims to reduce emissions by 50% through improved energy efficiency and renewable energy use.

### Application of best practices

One of the key activities is to develop a strategy for the application of best practices from ground-breaking projects in the region and further afield. One area that has been identified as a priority is the development of a strategy for the application of best practices from the MEA (Monitoring, Evaluation, and Adaptation) project. The project aims to promote the use of best practices from the MEA project and to develop a strategy for the application of these best practices in the region.

The project will also include the development of a strategy for the application of best practices from other projects in the region. This includes the development of a strategy for the application of best practices from the MEA project and the development of a strategy for the application of best practices from other projects in the region.

### Project Details

#### Project 1
- **Description**: Implementation of renewable energy sources in rural communities.
- **Objective**: To reduce carbon footprint and increase energy efficiency.
- **Timeline**: 2 years.

#### Project 2
- **Description**: Development of green transportation infrastructure.
- **Objective**: To promote eco-friendly travel options for residents.
- **Timeline**: 18 months.

#### Project 3
- **Description**: Establishment of urban green spaces.
- **Objective**: To improve air quality and provide recreational areas.
- **Timeline**: 24 months.

### Funding Allocation

- **Government Grant** (50,000 USD): Allocated to Project 1 for renewable energy implementation.
- **Private Donation** (30,000 USD): Allocated to Project 2 for green transportation infrastructure.
- **Corporate Sponsorship** (70,000 USD): Allocated to Project 3 for urban green spaces establishment.

### Additional Notes

- The funding will be used to cover initial setup costs and operational expenses for the first year.
- Regular progress reports will be submitted to the funding sources.
- Any unused funds will be reallocated to support future sustainability projects.

This proposal is based on the needs of over 100 countries involved in each submission group. Each option is a viable low-carbon development path with significant co-benefits for sustainable development. The proposal aims to enhance the effectiveness, efficiency, and sustainability of ESA-related activities and national resources for enhanced climate resilience.

The proposal also aims to strengthen the capacity of ESA to provide accurate and timely information on climate impacts and vulnerabilities. This will enable countries to better prepare for and respond to the effects of climate change, while also promoting sustainable development.

The project will be implemented over a period of five years, with the following milestones:

- **Year 1:** Development of the proposal and engagement with relevant stakeholders.
- **Year 2:** Conducting vulnerability assessments and developing strategies and action plans.
- **Year 3:** Implementation of specific adaptation measures in selected countries.
- **Year 4:** Strengthening regional cooperation and establishing a regional network.
- **Year 5:** Finalization of all activities and reporting on project outcomes.

The monitoring and evaluation framework will include regular progress reports, stakeholder consultations, and an independent evaluation at the end of the project.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2 | PAGE 77 OF 88

## Section 1: Executive Summary

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a unique global platform to respond to climate change by supporting action in developing countries. Established in 2010, the GCF aims to promote a low-emission and climate-resilient development pathway.

### Objective
- Mobilize $100 billion per year by 2020 for climate action.
- Support mitigation and adaptation projects.
- Foster climate resilience and reduce vulnerability.

## Section 2: Project Proposal

### Activity 1.1: Renewable Energy Projects
- **Objective**: Transition to renewable energy sources.
- **Actions**:
  - Implement solar power projects in rural areas.
  - Develop wind farms along coastal regions.
  - Promote energy efficiency programs for households and businesses.

### Activity 1.2: Ecosystem Restoration
- **Objective**: Restore degraded ecosystems.
- **Actions**:
  - Plant trees to restore deforested areas.
  - Implement mangrove restoration projects.
  - Support sustainable agriculture practices.

### Activity 1.3: Climate Resilience Programs
- **Objective**: Enhance climate resilience in vulnerable communities.
- **Actions**:
  - Develop early warning systems for natural disasters.
  - Promote climate-smart agriculture.
  - Build resilient infrastructure, including flood defenses and drought-resistant crops.

## Section 3: Funding Requirements

### Budget Allocation
- Renewable Energy Projects: $50 million
- Ecosystem Restoration: $25 million
- Climate Resilience Programs: $25 million

### Co-financing Agreements
- Secure co-financing from international donors.
- Leverage private sector investments.
- Partner with local governments and NGOs.

## Section 4: Implementation Plan

### Timeline
- **Phase I (Year 1)**: Initial planning and project setup.
- **Phase II (Years 2-3)**: Project implementation.
- **Phase III (Year 4)**: Monitoring and evaluation.

### Milestones
- Complete feasibility studies by Q2 2023.
- Begin construction of renewable energy projects by Q4 2023.
- Achieve full ecosystem restoration by end of 2025.

## Section 5: Risk Management

### Potential Risks
- **Financial**: Insufficient funding or co-financing issues.
- **Technical**: Delays in project implementation.
- **Environmental**: Unforeseen environmental impacts.

### Mitigation Strategies
- Diversify funding sources to ensure financial stability.
- Implement rigorous project management to avoid delays.
- Conduct thorough environmental impact assessments.

## Section 6: Monitoring and Evaluation

### Indicators
- Number of renewable energy projects completed.
- Area of ecosystems restored.
- Number of communities with enhanced climate resilience.

### Reporting
- Annual progress reports submitted to GCF.
- Regular updates to stakeholders and partners.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2 | PAGE 19 OF 80

## Section 1: Introduction to Proposal

### 1.1 Purpose of Document
This document outlines the proposed funding strategy for addressing climate change through various initiatives.

### 1.2 Objectives
- To reduce greenhouse gas emissions.
- To promote renewable energy adoption.
- To support sustainable development practices.

### 1.3 Scope and Limitations
The proposal covers global initiatives with a focus on developing countries. It does not include detailed project plans or financial breakdowns.

## Section 2: Funding Allocation

### 2.1 General Guidelines
Funding will be allocated based on the following criteria:
- Environmental impact.
- Feasibility of implementation.
- Potential for replication and scaling.

### 2.2 Specific Projects

#### 2.2.1 Renewable Energy Initiatives
- **Solar Power Projects**: Installation of solar panels in rural areas.
  - **Objective**: To provide clean energy access to remote communities.
  - **Funding Required**: $500,000.

- **Wind Farm Development**: Establishment of wind farms in coastal regions.
  - **Objective**: To generate renewable electricity for urban centers.
  - **Funding Required**: $2,000,000.

#### 2.2.2 Energy Efficiency Programs
- **Building Retrofits**: Upgrading existing buildings to improve energy efficiency.
  - **Objective**: To reduce energy consumption in residential and commercial structures.
  - **Funding Required**: $1,500,000.

- **Appliance Upgrades**: Replacing old appliances with energy-efficient models.
  - **Objective**: To lower household energy usage.
  - **Funding Required**: $750,000.

#### 2.2.3 Sustainable Agriculture
- **Organic Farming Practices**: Promoting organic farming methods to reduce chemical use.
  - **Objective**: To improve soil health and reduce environmental impact.
  - **Funding Required**: $1,200,000.

- **Agroforestry Systems**: Implementing agroforestry practices to sequester carbon.
  - **Objective**: To enhance biodiversity while capturing greenhouse gases.
  - **Funding Required**: $800,000.

#### 2.2.4 Forest Conservation
- **Reforestation Projects**: Planting trees in deforested areas.
  - **Objective**: To restore forest cover and absorb carbon dioxide.
  - **Funding Required**: $1,000,000.

- **Protected Areas**: Establishing protected areas to conserve biodiversity.
  - **Objective**: To safeguard ecosystems and mitigate climate change.
  - **Funding Required**: $1,500,000.

#### 2.2.5 Waste Management
- **Recycling Programs**: Implementing recycling initiatives in urban settings.
  - **Objective**: To reduce waste sent to landfills and conserve resources.
  - **Funding Required**: $600,000.

- **Waste-to-Energy Plants**: Constructing facilities to convert waste into energy.
  - **Objective**: To generate electricity from municipal solid waste.
  - **Funding Required**: $1,200,000.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v.2 | PAGE 8 OF 8

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND

### Objective
All activities developed for the Green Climate Fund will be designed to promote and protect the environment.

### Funding Priorities
- **Environmental Protection**: Projects that aim to reduce pollution, conserve natural resources, and promote sustainable practices.
- **Renewable Energy**: Initiatives focused on developing renewable energy sources such as solar, wind, and hydro power.
- **Climate Change Mitigation**: Programs aimed at reducing greenhouse gas emissions through various means.

### Specific Objectives
1. **Reduce Pollution**: Implement projects that reduce air and water pollution in urban areas.
2. **Conserve Biodiversity**: Develop programs to protect endangered species and their habitats.
3. **Promote Sustainable Agriculture**: Support farming practices that are environmentally friendly and sustainable.
4. **Develop Renewable Energy**: Invest in the development of renewable energy sources such as solar, wind, and hydro power.
5. **Mitigate Climate Change**: Fund projects aimed at reducing greenhouse gas emissions through various means.

### Implementation Strategy
- **Identify Projects**: Conduct a thorough assessment to identify potential projects that align with funding priorities.
- **Develop Proposals**: Work with stakeholders to develop detailed proposals for identified projects.
- **Secure Funding**: Seek funding from various sources including government grants, private donations, and international organizations.
- **Monitor Progress**: Continuously monitor the progress of funded projects to ensure they meet their objectives.

### Budget Allocation
1. **Environmental Protection**: 30%
2. **Renewable Energy**: 40%
3. **Climate Change Mitigation**: 25%
4. **Administration**: 5%

### Monitoring and Evaluation
- **Baseline Assessments**: Conduct baseline assessments to measure the impact of funded projects.
- **Regular Reports**: Submit regular reports on project progress and outcomes.
- **Impact Assessments**: Perform impact assessments to evaluate the long-term effects of funded projects.

### Conclusion
The Green Climate Fund aims to promote environmental protection, renewable energy development, and climate change mitigation through strategic funding and implementation of various projects. By achieving these objectives, the fund will contribute significantly to global efforts in addressing environmental challenges.

## Activity 4: Financing mechanisms for climate change adaptation and mitigation

### The EFA funding facility at a glance

- **Objective**: To support developing countries in their efforts to address the adverse effects of climate change on health.
- **Target groups**: Developing countries, particularly those most vulnerable to climate change impacts.

### Activity 5: Technical assistance for climate change adaptation and mitigation

#### The EFA funding facility at a glance

- **Objective**: To provide technical assistance to developing countries in their efforts to adapt to and mitigate the effects of climate change.
- **Target groups**: Developing countries, particularly those most vulnerable to climate change impacts.

## National EFA Facility

### 4.1. Objective

The objective is to support the development and implementation of national health policies and strategies that address the adverse effects of climate change on health.

### 4.2. Components

- **Component 1**: Support for the development of national health policies and strategies.
- **Component 2**: Technical assistance for the implementation of these policies and strategies.
- **Component 3**: Capacity building for health professionals in climate change adaptation and mitigation.

### 4.3. Eligibility criteria

- **Eligible countries**: All developing countries, particularly those most vulnerable to climate change impacts.
- **Eligible activities**: Development and implementation of national health policies and strategies, technical assistance, capacity building.

### 4.4. Implementation arrangements

- **Project cycle**: The project will follow a standard project cycle, including preparation, implementation, monitoring, and evaluation.
- **Coordination**: The facility will coordinate with other relevant international organizations and initiatives to ensure effective use of resources and avoid duplication.

## Global EFA Facility

### 5.1. Objective

The objective is to support the development and implementation of global health policies and strategies that address the adverse effects of climate change on health.

### 5.2. Components

- **Component 1**: Support for the development of global health policies and strategies.
- **Component 2**: Technical assistance for the implementation of these policies and strategies.
- **Component 3**: Capacity building for global health professionals in climate change adaptation and mitigation.

### 5.3. Eligibility criteria

- **Eligible countries**: All developing countries, particularly those most vulnerable to climate change impacts.
- **Eligible activities**: Development and implementation of global health policies and strategies, technical assistance, capacity building.

### 5.4. Implementation arrangements

- **Project cycle**: The project will follow a standard project cycle, including preparation, implementation, monitoring, and evaluation.
- **Coordination**: The facility will coordinate with other relevant international organizations and initiatives to ensure effective use of resources and avoid duplication.

## Additional funding mechanisms

### 6.1. Global Environment Facility (GEF)

- **Objective**: To provide grants and low-interest loans for climate change adaptation and mitigation projects in developing countries.
- **Target groups**: Developing countries, particularly those most vulnerable to climate change impacts.
- **Eligible activities**: A wide range of activities related to climate change adaptation and mitigation.

### 6.2. Green Climate Fund (GCF)

- **Objective**: To support the development and implementation of climate change adaptation and mitigation projects in developing countries.
- **Target groups**: Developing countries, particularly those most vulnerable to climate change impacts.
- **Eligible activities**: A wide range of activities related to climate change adaptation and mitigation.

### 6.3. Adaptation Fund

- **Objective**: To support the development and implementation of climate change adaptation projects in developing countries.
- **Target groups**: Developing countries, particularly those most vulnerable to climate change impacts.
- **Eligible activities**: A wide range of activities related to climate change adaptation.

## E.7. Monitoring, reporting and evaluation arrangements

### 180

The Regional Manager (RM) will be responsible for the implementation of programme monitoring and evaluation (M&E). The RM will ensure that all implementing entities (IEs) are complying with the M&E requirements as per the Regional M&E Specialized Unit (RMSU) guidelines. The RMSU will provide technical support to IEs in developing their M&E plans, including indicators and data collection tools. The RMSU will also track the programme's progress towards achieving its goals.

### 185

The RM and RMSU will be responsible for the overall monitoring of the programme. They will ensure that all IEs are implementing their M&E plans effectively and efficiently. The RM will provide regular updates to the Green Climate Fund (GCF) on the programme's progress, including any challenges or issues encountered.

### 190

The Regional M&E Specialist and GCF will perform regular visits, at least once a year to each country where the programme is implemented. These visits aim to assess the effectiveness of the M&E system in place and provide technical support as needed. The RMSU will coordinate with IEs to ensure that all necessary data are collected and reported accurately.

### 195

CAME: In the second year, the Regional M&E Specialist (RM) will perform compliance reviews twice a year for programme implementation units. These reviews will assess whether the IEs are complying with CAME's guidelines and requirements. The RM will also provide technical support to IEs in improving their M&E systems.

### 200

As per the Accreditation Master Agreement (AMA), an independent consultant will be contracted to perform annual audits of the programme's financial statements and reports. These audits will ensure that all funds are used appropriately and that the programme is achieving its objectives as set out in the AMA. The RMSU will coordinate with GCF accredited entities and national audit teams to ensure the integrity of the programme's financial management.

### 205

The RM and RMSU will be responsible for ensuring that all IEs comply with the M&E requirements outlined in this proposal. They will also provide technical support to IEs in improving their M&E systems and data collection tools.

## 2. Gender mainstreaming and action plan

### 205.
During programme preparation, a gender assessment was conducted to understand the different gender needs and impacts of climate change at women and men in Dr Congo and Zambia of the Green Climate Fund (GCF) project. The assessment identified key gender issues and challenges that affect women and men differently. It also highlighted the need for a gender-responsive approach to ensure equal access to benefits, opportunities, and resources. This will help to achieve gender equality and empower women.

### 206.
Traditional gender roles and activities are often affected by climate change. For example, in Zambia, women are responsible for collecting water and firewood, which can be impacted by droughts and floods. Climate change can exacerbate existing inequalities and increase the burden on women. It is important to consider these impacts when designing climate change projects.

### 207.
The costs of preparing this report have been covered by the project funds, with women actively participating in the migration process rather than passive actors relative to the households or communities they belong to. This has helped to ensure that their needs and concerns are addressed.

### 208.
Although community-based organizations have been involved in the assessment and can be the focal points for gender mainstreaming, it is important to recognize that women experience climate change differently from men. Women's experiences and needs should be taken into account when designing and implementing projects.

### 209.
Regarding direct damage, conclusions included in the assessment demonstrated the effects of climate change on women's livelihoods. For example, women are more likely to work in agriculture and fishing for under ten hours a day, which makes them more vulnerable to climate-related disasters such as floods, droughts, and storms. Women also face additional challenges due to their roles as caregivers, which can limit their ability to respond to climate change.

### 210.
The assessment demonstrates that women are often the primary producers of food but have limited access to resources such as land, credit, and technology. This limits their ability to adapt to climate change. Women also face cultural barriers that prevent them from participating in decision-making processes related to climate change.

### 211.
The assessment describes the outcomes and recommendations derived from the project will enhance programme implementation activities. We will develop a gender-responsive plan for women's participation in climate change projects. This includes training women on climate change adaptation strategies, providing access to credit and technology, and promoting women's leadership in climate change initiatives.

### 212.
The assessment also highlights the need for continued support from the Green Climate Fund (GCF) and other partners to ensure that gender-responsive approaches are integrated into all aspects of programme implementation. This will help to achieve the goals of the project and promote gender equality and empowerment of women in climate change initiatives.

## Development and Salary of all Reports prepared will GCF is under a proper monitoring and evaluation system.

### Regarding the Humanity Complex, CAAREI is an international Development Bank, which has been established in accordance with the provisions of the Multilateral Agreement on the Establishment of CAAREI (MAECA).

### Policy and Procedures Manual is updated and distributed to all staff and external operations, and in accordance with the requirements of the GCF. The manual will be reviewed annually by the Board of Directors and will be made available to all staff and in accordance with the latest international standards. This includes and ensures that CAAREI is fully compliant with the requirements of the GCF. The manual will also ensure that the policies and procedures are updated and revised as necessary, and that the programs are administered in accordance with the requirements of the GCF.

### CAAREI will further review its policies to ensure a consistent monitoring and evaluation of activities that form part of an overall country program. These internal activities have been reviewed and approved by the Board of Directors.

### CAAREI has a Risk Rating System, which is comprised of comprehensive and periodic reviews of all programs and operations.

### CAAREI has a Standard Claims Chart for the settlement of all contingencies. This Google tool is used to ensure that all claims are properly processed and settled in accordance with the terms and conditions of the contract.

### On the other hand, CAAREI has implemented several mechanisms such as audit, verification, and monitoring systems to ensure compliance with the requirements of the GCF. These include internal audits, external audits, and third-party audits. CAAREI can be audited by any of these methods at any time.

### The Integrity and Compliance Office is in charge of ensuring and imposing a culture of integrity and ethical behavior to all employees and stakeholders. This office will also ensure that the internal control system is properly implemented and maintained, and that it is compliant with international standards.

### As mentioned by the status in an Accredited Entity to the GCF, CAAREI has demonstrated expertise in project preparation, implementation, and management of projects funded by the GCF.

### The approval of all funds by CAAREI will be subject to the approval of the Board of Directors. All actions undertaken by the Board of Directors are in accordance with the provisions of the MAECA. The Board of Directors is responsible for ensuring that the funds are used in accordance with the approved budget and in compliance with the requirements of the GCF.

## CARE's Role
CARE will determine the highest order standards during the procurement and evaluation of the proposals. CARE will ensure that the proposals are evaluated based on their alignment with the objectives, in particular relevant sub-objectives and core values. Choice of the “best” proposal is dependent upon the implementation-organizational capacity to be able to implement the project.

### 9.1 Disclosure of funding proposals
CARE will provide that the GCF will only consider proposals in direct response to an invitation for proposals issued by CARE. Proposals received outside this process will not be considered. CARE will ensure that all proposals are treated equally and fairly, and that the selection process is transparent and accountable.

### 9.2 No Confidentiality
The detailed funding proposal is being provided to the GCF for informational purposes only. The information may be disclosed in whole or in part by the GCF, as the information is being provided in confidence. Accordingly, the accredited entity should not rely on any information contained herein and should seek independent legal advice.

All copies of confidential proposals are marked accordingly. Confidentiality is strictly maintained. No disclosure is made to third parties without prior written consent from CARE. The information provided on this occasion does not confer any rights or obligations on either party, and no contract will be formed unless in writing signed by both parties.

Dear Madam/Sir,

We refer to the aforementioned Forest-based Adaptation to increase climate resilience in the Central American Dry Corridor and the Arid Zones of the Dominican Republic in Guatemala, which was submitted by Central American Bank for Economic Integration (CABEI) on November 27th, 2018.

The undertaking is the daily administrative responsibility of the Minister of Environment and Natural Resources, Mario Roberto Rojas López, in his capacity as NDA of Guatemala.

Pursuant to GCF decision B.09/16, the contents of which we acknowledge to have reviewed, we hereby submit our approval for funding to support the increase of climate resilience in the Central American Dry Corridor and the Arid Zones of the Dominican Republic included in the detailed proposal.

(b) The Government of Guatemala has no objection to the programme Forest-based Adaptation to Increase Climate Resilience in the Central American Dry Corridor and the Arid Zones of the Dominican Republic as included in the funding proposal, which is being submitted by CABEI on behalf of Guatemala.

Please find attached the official letter of endorsement for the programme "Economy-based Adaptation to increase climate resilience in the General American Dry Corridor and the Arid Zones of the Dominican Republic", submitted by the Central American Bank for Economic Integration (CABEI) on November 29th, 2018.

# YANNICK GLEMAREC

Great Officer Fund

**Re: Tender presented for the GCF by Central American Bank for Economic Integration (CABEI) to support the implementation of the Central American Strategy for Comprehensive Risk Management and the Red Zones of the Dominican Republic.**

Our Ref: Gl/2021/09/15

Paris, September 3rd, 2021

SUBJECT: GCF FUNDING PROPOSAL

Dear Mr. Glemarec,

We are pleased to present you with the Funding Proposal submitted by the Central American Bank for Economic Integration (CABEI) to support the implementation of the Central American Strategy for Comprehensive Risk Management and the Red Zones of the Dominican Republic.

The objective of this proposal is to provide funding to CABEI in order to implement the project "Support to the Government of the Dominican Republic in the Implementation of the National Policy for Disaster Risk Reduction (NDP-RR)".

Presented to GCF Board 31, this project aims to strengthen the capacities of local governments and communities to reduce disaster risk. It is aligned with the objectives of the GCF Strategic Programme on Risk Management.

In our continuing support and cooperation, it is emphasized that:

1. The government of Panama has submitted its application to the European Commission's "European Fund for Sustainable Development" (EFSD) to finance climate resilience in the Central American Dry Corridor.
2. The project aims to strengthen the capacities of local governments and communities to reduce disaster risk.
3. The project is aligned with the objectives of the GCF Strategic Programme on Risk Management.
4. The project is consistent with the objectives of the National Policy for Disaster Risk Reduction (NDP-RR) of Panama.

We are confident that our collaboration will enable all proposed activities to be implemented within the scope of this proposal.

We acknowledge the technical assistance available from the GCF Secretariat.

Yours sincerely,

**MILCO GARCIA CONCEPCION**

Assistant General Manager
National Development Fund
Republic of Panama

# Secretariat’s assessment of FP174

## Proposal name:
Economic and Social Adaptation to Increase Climate Resilience to Strengthen Food Security in the Dry Corridor of Central America (ESCAME)

## Received from:
Country(ies): Costa Rica, El Salvador, Guatemala, Honduras, Nicaragua
Programme: P3 and Domestic Republics

## Programme/ programme size:
Large

## L. Overall assessment of the Secretariat

### Strengths
- The programme aligns with the objectives of the Green Climate Fund (GCF) to support climate resilience for food security.
- The programme will encourage participation of local governments, private sector, and civil society organizations in addressing environmental issues.
- The technical assistance facility is designed to strengthen technical capacities at the national level to implement and monitor activities.
- The programme has a well-defined budget that includes a rigorous monitoring and evaluation system.

### Weaknesses
- The programme lacks a regional vulnerability assessment that includes climate change impacts, which could affect the effectiveness of the project.
- The implementation flexibility module should be strengthened to ensure that it is aligned with the objectives of the programme.

### Points of caution
- The implementation arrangements will need to be carefully monitored to ensure that they are consistent and coherent across countries.
- GCF and CARE will need to be vigilant in ensuring that the programme is implemented effectively, and that lessons learned will be further defined for the programme.

### Recommendations
- This programme is likely to have a greater focus on climate resilience for regional food security, and will require additional resources to achieve its objectives.


---
**Page 116**
---

# IL - Summary of the Secretary’s assessment

## 2.1 Programme background

### i. Background/history of the programme to inform assessment of the funding request

The programme aims to consider approaches that could address the root causes and conditions that fuel the recent surge in illegal, unreported and unregulated (IUU) fishing. Listed below are proposed conditions and

# Green Climate Fund

## Presentation

Presentations indicate that by the end of the century, temperatures will increase by 3-5.5°C under Representative Concentration Pathway 4.5 and by 6-7°C under RCP8.5. Under these scenarios, precipitation is expected to change by 10% per degree Celsius. These changes will be compounded by further changes in the Boreal region rainfall events across the region. If average global temperatures reach 1.5°C above pre-industrial levels, extreme weather events such as heatwaves, droughts, and heavy precipitation are expected to increase by 20 years, and these severe components for agriculture and forestry will be exacerbated.

- The introduction of new species into the region is likely to have a significant impact on agricultural productivity.
- Introduction of foreign species and vegetative barriers, which would lead to an increase in the number of invasive species.
- The introduction of new species will be responsive to the above-described climate change.

# Green Infrastructure Code

## Introduction

Community-specific implementation protocols will be downloaded for the implementation of natural drainage solutions. These protocols include:

1. Solutions for water-retaining technology;
2. Solutions for water quality improvement; and
3. Solutions for water quantity management.

### a) A USD 70 million grant facility will be established to support site preparation, design, and construction of green infrastructure projects. This will also provide access to finance. Based on approved eligibility criteria, a selected set of interventions will be implemented in the pilot phase. These include:

- Community-based natural solutions including non-structural, rainwater harvesting, and water storage;
- Natural solutions for water quality improvement such as constructed wetlands, riparian buffers, and green roofs;
- Water efficiency technologies.

- Development of a web-based platform to provide access to information, tools, and resources.

# Green Climate Fund

## GCF-012/Rev.03

### Introduction

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a new international financing mechanism to support mitigation and adaptation actions in developing countries.

### Objectives

- To assist countries in achieving their climate change objectives.
- To promote a low-emission, climate-resilient development pathway.
- To support the implementation of country-driven strategies and actions for low-emission and climate-resilient development.

### Key Features

- GCF will support projects, programmes, policies, and other activities in developing countries using simplified approval processes.
- GCF will promote a country-led approach and support regional initiatives.
- GCF will maintain a balance between mitigation and adaptation, with a majority of resources devoted to adaptation in the early years.

### Governance Structure

GCF is governed by a Board composed of 24 members, equally divided between developed and developing countries. The Board has established various committees to oversee different aspects of GCF operations.

### Funding

GCF aims to mobilize $100 billion per year by 2020 from both public and private sources. The Fund will provide grants and concessional loans to support climate change actions in developing countries.

### Operational Priorities

- Support for country-driven strategies.
- Promotion of a low-emission, climate-resilient development pathway.
- Enhancement of resilience and reduction of vulnerability to climate change impacts.

# Green Climate Fund

## III. Assessment of performance against investment criteria

### 2.1 Impact potential

The programme will contribute to sustainable water supply and irrigation, agroforestry, integrated soil fertility management, and climate-smart agriculture. It will directly benefit 30,000 smallholder farmers (15% women) through improved agricultural productivity and increased income. The programme will also support the development of local markets for agricultural products, creating jobs and increasing incomes.

The programme will contribute to the achievement of the following Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs):

- **SDG 6**: Clean water and sanitation
- **SDG 8**: Decent work and economic growth
- **SDG 13**: Climate action

### Scale: Medium to High

The programme will directly benefit 30,000 smallholder farmers (15% women) through improved agricultural productivity and increased income. The programme will also support the development of local markets for agricultural products, creating jobs and increasing incomes.

The programme will contribute to the achievement of the following Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs):

- **SDG 6**: Clean water and sanitation
- **SDG 8**: Decent work and economic growth
- **SDG 13**: Climate action

### 2.2 Gender equality

The programme includes the development of agro-ecological intensification approaches that are gender-responsive, ensuring equal access to resources and opportunities for women and men. It will also support the establishment of gender-responsive value chains and market systems.

Furthermore, the programme includes specific adaptation plans that will be developed and adapted to address the needs of particularly vulnerable groups in the one hundred villages targeted by the programme. These plans will target actions and interventions aimed at reducing pressure on ecosystems, and the services they provide, while also promoting sustainable livelihoods.

With an estimated budget of USD 14 million (USD 8.6 million for the Green Climate Fund contribution and USD 5.4 million co-financing), the programme is designed to be implemented over a period of five years.

# Green Climate Fund

## Integrated Approach to Promote Sustainable Land Management

Degraded agricultural land restored through the implementation of sustainable land use practices (i.e., ~280ha of degraded area restored) (~1,000ha of forest protected) (~3) new tree nurseries established and maintained (~500ha of degraded land restored) (~400ha of degraded land restored with improved water harvesting systems). EIA community-based natural resource management will be strengthened through the establishment of a community-based natural resource management system. The proposed integrated approach to promote sustainable land management in the Dryland Ecosystem will be implemented.

### Paradigm Shift Potential

The proposed program will catalyze a paradigm shift in the regional approach to sustainable development by promoting an integrated and holistic approach to ecosystem restoration. This will be supported through the implementation of the Dryland Restoration Initiative, which aims to promote sustainable land management practices that are integrated with other interventions to build community capacity who would own and benefit from the enhanced resources, and to promote the adoption of best practices with the aim of achieving sustainable development outcomes.

The program has been specifically designed to create an enabling environment that supports the sustainability of EIA focusing on three core areas of support: (i) policy advocacy; (ii) capacity building; and (iii) community engagement. The program will be implemented through a combination of direct interventions, technical assistance, and capacity building activities.

1. **Policy Advocacy**

The enhanced toolkit developed for EIA law will be created through the knowledge management system that will support the development of evidence-based policy advocacy materials to inform policy and promote the use of EIA in protected areas.

2. **Capacity Building**

Local landowners will be trained on sustainable land management practices, including the use of improved water harvesting systems and other technologies to enhance their capacity to manage their lands sustainably.

3. **Community Engagement**

The program will engage local communities in the implementation of sustainable land management practices through community-based natural resource management initiatives that promote the conservation of biodiversity and the sustainable use of natural resources.

### Sustainable Development Potential

The program will have significant sustainable development potential with improvements in water, food, and energy security beneficial to the environment. The implementation of this initiative will contribute to the achievement of several SDGs including:

- **SDG 1: No Poverty** - by improving livelihoods through increased agricultural productivity.
- **SDG 6: Clean Water and Sanitation** - by improving water management practices.
- **SDG 7: Affordable and Clean Energy** - by promoting the use of renewable energy sources.
- **SDG 13: Climate Action** - by reducing greenhouse gas emissions through improved land management practices.

The program will also contribute to the achievement of several other SDGs, including but not limited to:

- **SDG 2: Zero Hunger**
- **SDG 8: Decent Work and Economic Growth**
- **SDG 9: Industry, Innovation, and Infrastructure**
- **SDG 15: Life on Land**

The program will also contribute to the achievement of several other SDGs, including but not limited to:

- **SDG 2: Zero Hunger**
- **SDG 8: Decent Work and Economic Growth**
- **SDG 9: Industry, Innovation, and Infrastructure**
- **SDG 15: Life on Land**

# Green Climate Fund

## Targeted interventions to enhance health by reducing the risk of water-borne diseases, increased productivity of agricultural outputs, and reduced inequality and poverty. Enhanced resilience to climate change will provide opportunities to develop sustainable NDCs and alternative livelihoods in vulnerable communities.

### Needs of the recipient  Scale: Medium to high
- With limited access to clean water, sanitation, and carbon-free energy, the region has achieved an average annual economic growth rate of 3.2% per year over the past decade. However, this has been accompanied by increased vulnerability to climate-related risks such as flooding, droughts, and fires. Climate change impacts include higher temperatures, more frequent and intense heatwaves, and changes in precipitation patterns, which have led to an increase in water-borne diseases, while limiting their adaptive capacity. Improving access to clean water and sanitation is critical for enhancing community resilience to climate change. Investments in Dry Corridor will be increased. With increased funding of new technical assistance projects, the region has been able to improve its ability to adapt to climate change.

- The national budget of Dry Corridor countries is limited, and private sector engagement and sustainable development opportunities have been prioritized for climate risk CCA. There is a high demand for climate finance from the public sector. The Green Climate Fund (GCF) will provide support to the region through its enhanced direct access modality. This will enable the region to implement projects that address the needs of vulnerable communities.

### Country ownership  Scale: Medium to high
- GCF is recognized as a priority in the Central American region and has been mainstreamed into several regional and national policies and strategies, including the Regional Green Climate Fund (GCF) Readiness Program, National Adaptation Plans (NAPs), Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs), and National Adaptation Strategies. The GCF Readiness Program is a key component of the region's climate change strategy.

- In addition to its regional alignment, the program contributes to achieving national priorities. The program will support the development of a regional NAP process that aligns with the Paris Agreement. It will also support the development of a regional adaptation plan and provide technical assistance to countries in the region. The program will also support the development of a regional adaptation plan and provide technical assistance to countries in the region.

### Efficiency and effectiveness  Scale: Medium to high
- The program provides comprehensive demand and supply analysis to identify the most effective climate change interventions for the region. It also supports the development of an efficient and effective monitoring, evaluation, and learning system that will enable the program to achieve its objectives. The program will also support the development of a regional adaptation plan and provide technical assistance to countries in the region.

- The program provides comprehensive demand and supply analysis to identify the most effective climate change interventions for the region. It also supports the development of an efficient and effective monitoring, evaluation, and learning system that will enable the program to achieve its objectives. The program will also support the development of a regional adaptation plan and provide technical assistance to countries in the region.

# Green Climate Fund

## Programs providing sufficient evidence that there is growing interest to engage in green finance by financial institutions and that the financial engagement is very likely to be sustained over time

### Green Finance Program (GFP)

The GFP aims to support the development of a green finance market through the provision of technical assistance. The program has supported 15 projects, at an average cost of USD 89,063.11. GFP increased loan portfolios by an average of 24% and improved the quality of loans by 17%. The program also facilitated the development of green finance policies and standards in several countries.

### Green Bond Program (GBP)

The GBP aims to support the issuance of green bonds, which are financial instruments that raise funds for environmentally friendly projects. The program has supported 20 projects, at an average cost of USD 145,376.89. GBP increased loan portfolios by an average of 35% and improved the quality of loans by 22%. The program also facilitated the development of green bond standards in several countries.

## Assessment of consistency with GCF safeguards and policies

### Environmental and social safeguards

#### Programme background

The programs aim to promote the benefits to the environment and society from green finance. The programs include technical assistance, capacity building, and policy dialogue. The programs also support the development of environmental and social management systems.

- The programs have no-financing of up to 65%. The large concentrations will enable financial institutions to increase their lending for green projects.
- The programs aim to promote sustainable finance by providing technical assistance, capacity building, and policy dialogue.
- The programs aim to support the development of environmental and social management systems.

- Number of projects supported
- Increase in loan portfolios

## ESMF - Executive Summary

The European Standardisation Organisation (ESMO) has been working on a comprehensive framework for the development of environmental management systems. The programme is designed to provide a coherent approach to the implementation of appropriate standards. This will be achieved effectively through the integration of appropriate, internationally recognised standards and guidelines.

### Compliance with GHS environmental and social safeguards (EHS) standards

The ESMF has been developed in collaboration with the European Commission and other stakeholders to ensure compliance with the GHS environmental and social safeguards (EHS) standards. This will enable organisations to meet their obligations under the GHS.

### ESMF - Land management of environmental and social impacts

The ESMF is designed to provide a framework for the management of environmental and social impacts. The programme has been developed in collaboration with the European Commission and other stakeholders to ensure compliance with the GHS environmental and social safeguards (EHS) standards. This will enable organisations to meet their obligations under the GHS.

### ESMF - Labour and working conditions

The programme also provides employment opportunities for local communities through the implementation of the programme. The ESMF has been developed in collaboration with the European Commission and other stakeholders to ensure compliance with the GHS environmental and social safeguards (EHS) standards. This will enable organisations to meet their obligations under the GHS.

### ESMF - Resource efficiency and pollution prevention

The programme also provides employment opportunities for local communities through the implementation of the programme. The ESMF has been developed in collaboration with the European Commission and other stakeholders to ensure compliance with the GHS environmental and social safeguards (EHS) standards. This will enable organisations to meet their obligations under the GHS.

### ESMF - Community health, safety and security

The programme also provides employment opportunities for local communities through the implementation of the programme. The ESMF has been developed in collaboration with the European Commission and other stakeholders to ensure compliance with the GHS environmental and social safeguards (EHS) standards. This will enable organisations to meet their obligations under the GHS.

### ESMF - Land acquisition and industrial resettlement

The programme also provides employment opportunities for local communities through the implementation of the programme. conservation and restoration of the forest areas in the largest basins. The programme also aims to modification of current land-use practices and/or reduction of income from forest resources to ensure sustainable management of these resources. In addition, the programme will implement inclusive education and decision-making processes that empower local communities to participate in the planning and implementation of the proposed interventions. Further, the programme will support the development of a national strategy for the conservation and restoration of biodiversity.

### ESS - Biodiversity conservation and sustainable management of forest natural resources

The programme will focus on the conservation and sustainable management of forest natural resources through the promotion of biodiversity conservation, ecosystem restoration and rehabilitation, reforestation and afforestation of degraded lands, protection of endangered species, and enhancement of ecosystem services. The programme will also support the development of sustainable land-use practices that promote the conservation of biodiversity and ecosystem services in the targeted areas. In addition, the programme will support the development of policies and strategies for the conservation of biodiversity and ecosystem services, including the establishment of sustainable sources of livelihoods for local communities. The programme will also support the development of a national strategy for the conservation of biodiversity.

### GEF Indicator Performance Policy and ESS Indigenous peoples

The programme will be implemented in collaboration with indigenous peoples and local communities where the programme is to be implemented. The All programmes that have been identified as part of this programme are expected to be implemented in accordance with the GEF indicator performance policy and ESS indigenous peoples policy. The programme will also support the development of a national strategy for the conservation of biodiversity.

### ESS - Cultural heritage

Some of the regions where the programme will be implemented have significant cultural heritage sites that are important for the promotion of sustainable management of forest natural resources. The programme will support the development of policies and strategies for the protection and management of these cultural heritage sites. The programme will also support the development of a national strategy for the conservation of biodiversity.

# Implementation arrangements

## Implementation arrangements (CABR) for the IR will be responsible for ensuring the implementation of the programme. A Business Project Management Unit (BPMU) will be created to manage the implementation process. The BPMU will be responsible for the day-to-day administration, execution and management of technical supervision of project activities. The BPMU will report directly to the CABR. The Environmental and Social Safeguards Officer (ESSO) is expected to have a natural officer who will be responsible for ensuring that all environmental and social safeguard requirements are met throughout the life of the programme.

# 4.3 Risks (The risks of the environment have been rated and full assessment carried with the help on internal consultation teams)

## 4.4 Fellowship

In the EIA, the present are FAO, the Central American Commission for Environment and Development (CCAD) and CABRI, which will also perform the role of AE in the project.

The EIA will be implemented through the following actions: technical assistance and implementation of the EIA documentation; the strengthening of regional environmental management capacities; the development of a regional strategy to address and eliminate pollution; the promotion of sustainable development; and the establishment of an information system on environmental issues. The project will also promote the development of a regional strategy for the protection of natural resources, including the conservation of biodiversity.

In this line, as in CABRI it will be responsible for overseeing its implementation. Financial support from the Global Environment Facility (GEF) is expected to be available to support these activities.

The project will work with FAO Regional Fisheries and Aquaculture Commission (RPFC), commission of representatives from MA, CCAD, CABRI and the RFMP and the regional project manager (RPM). # The Accreditation Master Agreement was signed with the Accredited Entity on 6 September 2017 ("AMA"), and became effective on November 2017.

The Secretariat has been working closely with the Accredited Entity to ensure that it has obtained all relevant approvals, and it has the capacity and authority to implement the agreement. The Secretariat is also in regular contact with the Accredited Entity to monitor its progress towards implementing the agreement. The Secretariat will continue to provide support to the Accredited Entity as needed.

The proposed program will be implemented in several countries, not which six are on a grant period with previous and continuous, while in other countries it is a new program. The Secretariat has been working closely with the Accredited Entity to ensure that all necessary approvals have been obtained for the implementation of the program in each country.

## The Regional Coordination Unit

The Secretariat sent a draft agreement together with the Accredited Entity to the Regional Coordination Unit ("RCU") on 21 November 2017. The RCU has reviewed and approved the draft agreement, which was signed by both parties on 6 December 2017. The RCU will continue to work closely with the Secretariat and the Accredited Entity to ensure that all necessary approvals have been obtained for the implementation of the program in each country.

## The Domestic Agencies

The Secretariat sent a draft agreement together with the Accredited Entity to the Domestic Regulatory Agencies on 21 November 2017. The Secretariat and the Accredited Entity are working closely with the Domestic Regulatory Agencies to ensure that all necessary approvals have been obtained for the implementation of the program in each country.

## The Regional ILS Board

The Secretariat sent a draft agreement together with the Accredited Entity to the Regional ILS Board on 21 November 2017. The Secretariat and the Accredited Entity are working closely with the Regional ILS Board to ensure that all necessary approvals have been obtained for the implementation of the program in each country.

## The Regional Humanitarian

The Secretariat sent a draft agreement together with the Accredited Entity to the Humanitarian Coordination Unit on 21 November 2017. The Secretariat and the Accredited Entity are working closely with the Humanitarian Coordination Unit to ensure that all necessary approvals have been obtained for the implementation of the program in each country.

## The Regional GC

The Secretariat sent a draft agreement together with the Accredited Entity to the Global Coordination Unit on 21 November 2017. The Secretariat and the Accredited Entity are working closely with the Global Coordination Unit to ensure that all necessary approvals have been obtained for the implementation of the program in each country.

## The Regional GC Engagement

# September 2017

In October 2016, the Secretariat met with the NDA to provide information about the draft agreement and its status. The Secretariat has not yet received any further updates from the NDA.

## Background & Negotiations

The Secretariat sent a draft agreement to the NDA in April 2016. The NDA has been reviewing it internally since then, and has indicated that they would like to conduct a review of their approval by the Congress, which is a lengthy process. The NDA proposed the Secretariat transmit the proposal to Congress, however, no formal request has occurred at this time.

### Meetings with IBIA & IBA

The Head of the Independent Business Insights (IBI) and Independent Budgetary Analysis (IBA) units of the Secretariat met with representatives from the NDA in April 2017 to discuss the proposed agreement. The IBI and IBA units are responsible for providing independent analysis on the economic, social, and environmental impacts of agreements under negotiation by the Secretariat.

### Proposed Agreement

The proposed agreement establishes the identification of knowledge sharing be funded by a portion of the GCP funding and the Accredited Entity’s cost-sharing with each other. The Secretariat has indicated that it is willing to consider additional funding from the NDA to support greater technical assistance (PTA), which in turn would be used to support the proposed agreement. The Secretariat has also indicated that it is willing to consider a portion of the PTA for purposes of capacity building and support for the GCP, provided that the NDA does not use this funding to support the PTA. The Secretariat will work with the Accredited Entity to ensure that the conditions for such technical assistance are met.

### List of Proposal Conditions (including legal)

1. In order to recognize work, it is recommended that my agreement by the Board be submitted by the Accredited Entity to the Fund a certificate of legal opinion, in forms and substance satisfactory to the GCP Secretariat, within 120 days after Board approval.
2. The Accredited Entity shall have the opportunity to submit any additional information or documentation to support its legal opinion within 30 days from the date of Board approval, or on such later date as may be agreed upon by the Accredited Entity and the GCP Secretariat.
3. Completion of the legal due diligence is a condition precedent to the execution of the GCP Secretariat agreement.

# Independent Technical Advisory Panel’s assessment of FP174

## Proposal name:
Economic-based Adaptation to increase climate resilience in the General Areas, Dry Corridor and the Agro-Zones of the Central American Dry Corridor (CADC)

## Accredited entity:
Central American Bank for Economic Integration (CABEI)

## Countries/Programmes:
Costa Rica, El Salvador, Guatemala, Honduras, Nicaragua, Panama and Dominican Republic

## Programme/programme size:
Large

## I. Assessment of the Independent Technical Advisory Panel

1. Either versions of this funding proposal were submitted for review at the beauty pageant and beauty contest meetings of the Board (3.28 and 9.25), and did not receive the endorsement of the Board to proceed to implementation. The main reasons cited by the Board were related to the causal factors considered, specific activities selected, including:
   - The lack of a clear understanding of the problem
   - The absence of a theory of change
   - The absence of a monitoring framework where the Economic-based Adaptation (EBA) demonstration interventions and goals are clearly defined

2. Completion of a detailed-specific TOR plan for each of these seven countries:
   - Clear articulation of the adaptation needs by the project in per the GOI investment plans
   - Clear articulation of the relationship between the grant portion of the project, such as the technical assistance component, and the core investment program to be implemented directly by the Corridor

3. The assessment carried by the Independent Technical Advisory Panel (ITAP) is that the funding proposal has not provided sufficient time-specific data to support the theory of change. The lack of a clear understanding of the problem and the absence of a theory of change are areas where present information is insufficient to support the causal factors considered in the proposal. Therefore, it is recommended that the project be rejected.

## Impact potential

There is potential that the Central American Dry Corridor and the land zones of the Dominican Republic are among the world’s regions most vulnerable to the impacts of climate change. The region has been identified as a priority area for the implementation of climate change adaptation measures, including the use of climate risk insurance.

Special EBA plans for the zone enhance disaster risk reduction and the resilience of communities in the face of natural disasters. # Green Center for Science and Society

## Study Group Data Annotation Regarding the Habitats of Sandholder Farming Systems

Study group data annotation regarding the habitats of sandholder farming systems, and community projects and commercial farms were expected to assist these processes. This study was conducted in collaboration with the National Institute of Agricultural Sciences (NIAS) and the Korean Rural Economic Institute (KREI). The study aimed to understand the importance of biodiversity in agricultural ecosystems and to develop a standardized protocol for assessing biodiversity in agricultural habitats.

### Information from the Microfinance Network of Central America and the Caribbean

Information from the Microfinance Network of Central America and the Caribbean was used to assess the impact of microfinance on smallholder farmers. The study was conducted using data from 100 smallholder farmers, with a focus on understanding the role of microfinance in improving agricultural productivity and livelihoods.

### Methods

The methods for assessing biodiversity were based on the protocols developed by the National Institute of Agricultural Sciences (NIAS) and the Korean Rural Economic Institute (KREI). The study used a combination of field surveys and laboratory analyses to assess the diversity and abundance of species in agricultural habitats. The study also included an assessment of the impact of microfinance on smallholder farmers.

### Results

The results of the study showed that biodiversity was significantly higher in agricultural habitats than in non-agricultural habitats. The study also found that the use of pesticides and fertilizers had a negative impact on biodiversity. The study recommended that farmers adopt integrated pest management practices to reduce the use of chemicals and improve biodiversity.

### Discussion

The discussion highlighted the importance of biodiversity for sustainable agriculture. The study also emphasized the need for further research to understand the mechanisms underlying the relationship between biodiversity and agricultural productivity. The study recommended that farmers adopt practices that promote biodiversity, such as crop rotation and agroforestry.

### Conclusion

The conclusion emphasized the importance of biodiversity for sustainable agriculture. The study also highlighted the need for further research to understand the mechanisms underlying the relationship between biodiversity and agricultural productivity. The study recommended that farmers adopt practices that promote biodiversity, such as crop rotation and agroforestry.

# Green Climate Fund

## Introduction

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a global fund created to support the efforts of developing countries to combat climate change. It was established in 2010 as part of the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). The GCF aims to promote a paradigm shift to low-emission and climate-resilient development, with a specific focus on supporting vulnerable countries.

## Sustainable Development Potential

### Scale: High

Non-governmental questions around the climate-specific impacts of the funding proposal for the project are not included in this document. However, the project is expected to have significant positive impacts on sustainable development through its support for renewable energy and energy efficiency measures.

### Alignment with SDGs

#### Scale: Medium

The proposed program is consistent with the broader goals of the funding proposal if adequately planned and implemented. The project will contribute to the achievement of some of the 17 United Nations Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs), including:

- SDG 6: Clean water and sanitation
- SDG 7: Affordable and clean energy
- SDG 9: Industry, innovation, and infrastructure
- SDG 11: Sustainable cities and communities
- SDG 13: Climate action

### Economic Co-benefits

#### Scale: High

The program is intended to increase access to credit which in turn should further reduce financial barriers to implementing adaptation measures for the target region.

### Social Co-benefits

#### Scale: Medium

The proposed project is expected to yield substantial social co-benefits, mostly concentrated in rural communities by improving the water and food security of subsistence farmers.

### Environmental Co-benefits

#### Scale: High

The program is designed to reduce greenhouse gas emissions through the promotion of renewable energy and energy efficiency measures.
