## Gender-related on-benefits

### a. Women in Central America lead to hold communities together with their knowledge of the environment
Women are generally more involved in agricultural activities than men. They have a greater ability to manage water resources and forests. Their participation is essential for the success of reforestation projects. Women's involvement in forest management can be enhanced by providing them with access to land, credit and training. The farming communities depend on women for food production and marketing. Women are also responsible for the care of children and elderly people. They are the main beneficiaries of the project interventions.

### b. Taking a close on-benefits into account
The independent TAP considers the sustainable development goals (SDGs) as an important part of the project's benefits. The SDGs provide a framework for achieving sustainable development, including poverty reduction, gender equality and environmental protection. The project will contribute to the achievement of several SDGs, including:

- **SDG 1: No Poverty** - by providing employment opportunities in reforestation and agroforestry.
- **SDG 5: Gender Equality** - by promoting women's participation in decision-making processes related to forest management.
- **SDG 6: Clean Water and Sanitation** - by improving water resources through reforestation.
- **SDG 8: Decent Work and Economic Growth** - by creating jobs in the forestry sector.
- **SDG 13: Climate Action** - by contributing to climate change mitigation and adaptation.

## 1.5 Needs of the recipient

### a. Forest and agroforestry projects
The local communities of the Caribbean region face challenges such as deforestation, degradation of natural resources, and lack of sustainable livelihoods. These challenges are exacerbated by climate change, which affects the availability and quality of water resources. The project aims to address these issues by implementing forest and agroforestry projects that improve soil fertility, increase biodiversity, and provide sustainable sources of income for local communities.

### b. Development and environmental conflicts
The development of the Caribbean region has led to environmental conflicts over land use, water resources, and natural resources. These conflicts can be exacerbated by climate change, which affects the availability and quality of water resources. The project aims to address these conflicts by promoting sustainable forest management practices that benefit both local communities and the environment.

### c. At the local level, vulnerable rural communities do not have the financial resources
At the local level, vulnerable rural communities do not have the financial resources to invest in sustainable development projects. This lack of funding can lead to environmental degradation and poverty. The project aims to address this issue by providing financial assistance to these communities.

### d. The natural habitats of Dry Corridor countries are limited, and private protected areas
The natural habitats of Dry Corridor countries are limited, and private protected areas are rare. This makes it difficult for local communities to access and benefit from the natural resources they depend on. The project aims to address this issue by promoting sustainable forest management practices that benefit both local communities and the environment.

### e. The added benefits of the COVID-19 pandemic
The added benefits of the COVID-19 pandemic have highlighted the importance of sustainable development in the Caribbean region. The project will build on these benefits by promoting sustainable forest management practices that benefit both local communities and the environment.

# Green Climate Fund

## II. Overall remarks from the Independent Technical Advisory Panel

### a.

The AI states that the Dry Corridor suffers and would continue to suffer from the effects of climate change. The GCF should support the implementation of the NAPA, which includes specific climate change adaptation actions by providing a region-wide loan and technical assistance for the implementation of the NAPA. This will include the development of an investment plan and climate change adaptation approach, underpinned by a clear timeline rationale for implementation. The AI recommends that the GCF should support the government in the following ways as well as the society at large:

- Demonstrate how climate change risk impact the areas of intervention in the future and what are the potential solutions to address these risks.
- Develop a programmatic approach and the ability to execute it, which will include the development of an investment plan and climate change adaptation approach that is region-wide.

# The Accredited Early Childhood Environment Rating Scales (ECERS)

The Accredited Early Childhood Environment Rating Scales (ECERS) are a set of observation instruments designed to assess the quality of early childhood environments. They were developed by the Accreditation Council for Early Childhood Programs and are used internationally.

## The Accredited Early Childhood Environment Rating Scale (ECERS)

The ECERS is a comprehensive tool that evaluates various aspects of an early childhood environment, including:

- **Space and Furnishings**
- **Personal Care Routines**
- **Language-Reasoning**
- **Activities**
- **Interactions**

## The Accredited Early Childhood Environment Rating Scale (ECERS)

The ECERS is designed to assess the quality of care in early childhood settings. It includes a checklist that covers different areas such as:

- **Space and Furnishings**: Assesses the layout, cleanliness, and safety of the environment.
- **Personal Care Routines**: Evaluates how well the staff handles personal care tasks.
- **Language-Reasoning**: Looks at the quality of interactions and language use between staff and children.
- **Activities**: Reviews the types and quality of activities provided to the children.
- **Interactions**: Assesses the nature and quality of interactions between staff and children.

## The Accredited Early Childhood Environment Rating Scale (ECERS)

The ECERS is a valuable tool for early childhood educators, administrators, and policymakers. It provides a framework for assessing the quality of care in early childhood settings and can be used to identify areas for improvement.

# Response from the accredited entity to the Independent Technical Advisory Panel’s assessment (FP174)

**Proposal title:**
Enhancement and Adaptation to increase climate resilience in the Central American Dry Corridor and the Artibonite Basin of Haiti

**Accredited entity:**
Central American Bank for Economic Integration (CABEI)

**Country(ies):**
Guatemala, Honduras, Nicaragua, Panama and Dominican Republic

**Programme/programme size:**
Large

## Impact potential

Most direct climate-related propositions to 2050 and 100b have been included in the revised version that defines the trends of increased rainfall in the Dry Corridor region, emphasizing the need for adaptation measures. The proposed project will be implemented through a set of criteria for the validation of the selected EIAs. In terms of observed data, there are very limited figures and, with the exception to trigger mechanisms, proposals have been made to strengthen the monitoring system.

The pre-defined pilot interventions have been selected through a site-specific consultation process that includes local stakeholders and experts. The project will reduce climate risks as well as other disaster risks that determine the vulnerability of the selected communities. The Independent Technical Advisory Panel (ITAP) has recommended that the project should be designed to ensure that the proposed measures are aligned with the Independent technical advisory panel on climate change (ITAP) recommendations.

## Paradigm shift potential

As the ITAP points out, the real paradigm shift potential lies in the potential of this enhanced approach to increase resilience and adaptation. The project will provide a framework for the financial sector to adopt measures that can be both useful for FP1, and non-replicated elsewhere.

# The need for adaptation funding in the region is far larger than the actual size of this programme and countries are facing rapidly with bad consequences being paid of several years of underfunding. This programme aims to support countries to create a sustainable financing mechanism alongside the private sector participation to ensure long-term sustainability.

## Sustainable development potential

IEA evaluates under six criteria: appropriateness, highlights how the EBA interventions are implemented and highlights how the larger context in each country were selected based on the needs of the country. The programme is designed to support countries to achieve their sustainable development goals but other environmental, social, and economic co-benefits.

## Need of the region

IEA evaluates under six criteria: appropriateness, highlights how the EBA interventions are implemented and highlights how the larger context in each country were selected based on the needs of the country. The programme is designed to support countries to achieve their sustainable development goals but other environmental, social, and economic co-benefits.

The need for adaptation funding in the region is far larger than the actual size of this programme and countries are facing rapidly with bad consequences being paid of several years of underfunding. This programme aims to support countries to create a sustainable financing mechanism alongside the private sector participation to ensure long-term sustainability.

## Country ownership

Country ownership is indeed very high for this programme. It was initiated by the Council of Ministers and is implemented at the highest level of government. There is a key opportunity to the moment to align and address the development objectives of the National Development Plan and the priorities set out in the Paris Agreement.

The strong commitment that will be behind the execution of this programme will ensure an effective implementation and the achievement of the objectives of the NAPA.

## Efficiency and effectiveness

CARE considers that this intervention could be assessed at least as Medium High, since the value for money is high. The programme is preparing a comprehensive approach addressing all key elements in an integrated manner to achieve the development objective. The programme has a strong institutional framework including national authorities and a sustainable financial mechanism seeking the private sector participation.

# Overall remarks from the Independent Technical Advisory Panel

CARFI believes that a sound and well-aligned technical assistance instrument provides from the GF and its core role is to support finance sector development and enhancing the market environment for financial services in the region. The TA should be demand-driven, based on needs identified by the authorities and demand-driven funding mechanisms to scale adaptation investments in the regions.

Responding to IAP's recommendations in its previous assessment, CARFI scale adjustments to the TA program to ensure that it is responsive to the needs of all programme countries. Developing the first site-specific intervention plans for 2 countries, CARFI has also developed a monitoring framework to track progress against the country and guarantee components of the program. In addition, phased structures for the climate resilience and market adaptation of interventions were developed.

CARFI takes note of the established conditions from IAP to the enhancement of the proposal and will continue to ensure that these conditions are met in order to meet the objectives.

# Gender Analysis/Assessment

## Part I: Gender Analysis/Assessment

### 1) Context of the Field Condition

The social, political, economic and environmental conditions of the countries occupying the key locations examined the influence of Central America, remain a trigger for the female gender to migrate. The migration of women is not only due to the economic hardship that compels parents, children and youth to leave their homes in search of better opportunities but also due to the historical context of violence, discrimination, poverty and inequality that have defined structural interests, power and gender relations. These conditions are exacerbated by the impact of natural disasters, climate change and environmental degradation that affect women and men differently.

Most violence in these countries is generated by organized crime and equivalent gang groups and lesser “Youth gangs” with members and affiliates of great breadth. Displacement, migration, illegal immigration, drug trafficking, extortion, sexual exploitation, domestic violence, school violence, police abuse and corruption, and the lack of opportunities for young people are some of the factors that contribute to the increase in gender-based violence. The impact of these conditions is exacerbated by the lack of access to justice and the absence of effective mechanisms to address these issues.

According to the Central American Integration System (SICA), in Central American countries, the percentage of rural population varies from 38% in El Salvador to 52% in Honduras and Nicaragua. The proportion of indigenous people is between 6% in Guatemala and 40% in Belize. The average for the region is 17%. The rest of the rural population is composed mainly of mestizos, blacks, whites, and mixed race. In addition, there are high levels of poverty and inequality that affect women and men differently.

The impact of climate change has been exacerbated by the lack of opportunities for young people in rural areas. This has led to an increase in migration to urban centers, where the majority of the population lives. The lack of opportunities in rural areas has also led to an increase in migration to other countries in search of better opportunities.

### 2) Gender Disaggregation

Gender disaggregation is a crucial factor for understanding the gender-based violence that affects women and children. It is essential to understand the different forms of violence that affect women and men differently, as well as the different forms of discrimination that affect women and men differently. It is also important to understand the different forms of discrimination that affect women and men differently in order to develop effective strategies to address these issues.

Gender disaggregation is a crucial factor for understanding the gender-based violence that affects women and children. It is essential to understand the different forms of violence that affect women and men differently, as well as the different forms of discrimination that affect women and men differently. # Gender-based Violence in the Context of Health

Gender-based violence is a extreme form of gender inequality and affects over one third of women in their lifetime. It includes physical, sexual and emotional violence within intimate relationships. It takes many forms, but sexual, physical and emotional violence against women is a major public health problem. It has serious consequences for the woman's physical and mental health, her family life and social relationships.

This text of violence, which women are most likely to take into account with the framework of the areas of intervention, is the fact that women are the targeted population in the area of gender-based violence. It is important to note that the abuse of power and control is not always physical, but can also be emotional, psychological and financial.

The practice of bride kidnapping in dry corridor countries is particularly symbolic, as it is done through a challenge or race between the suitors and the bride's family members, organized crime, and other actors. This practice has been associated with violence against women and girls and has increased in recent years due to the impact of climate change, food insecurity, and economic exploitation. It also contributes to the perpetuation of gender inequality and discrimination.

Of the 25 most violent countries in the world, 18 are in Latin America and the Caribbean. According to the Global Peace Index of 2021, Guatemala is ranked as the 137th safest country in the world. ## Violence against women environment defended from the pressure of extensive companies projects

In some territories, there have been demands by the organizations of rural women, that some governments have responded to, in order to protect their rights and to promote actions against common crimes. However, these actions have not been sufficient to guarantee the rights of women and young girls in the environment.

There are large tracts of territory that have been handed over to commercial companies for mining exploitation, which has led to an increase in violence against women and young girls in the environment. According to the report "Violence against Women and Mining", in 2013, there were more than 500 complaints from women who had been victims of sexual abuse by mining workers.

According to the report of the Movement of Affected by Mining (MAM), in El Salvador, between 2006 and 2014, a total of 2.9 million hectares of land were affected by mining projects. In the same period, there were more than 300 complaints against companies that had violated the rights of women and girls. The report also indicated that the impact of mining on women's rights is compounded when the communities are in conflict with each other.

The report also indicates that the violence against women in the environment is aggravated by the lack of access to justice and the impunity of those responsible for the crimes committed.

# Feminization of migration

In the context of climate change, the migration of women is caused by a variety of phenomena. The most obvious one is the need to perceive higher income elsewhere. However, the feminization of migration is also due to the fact that women's productive roles in the household are changing. They have less time for domestic chores and more time for income-generating activities. This has led to an increase in the number of female migrants. The feminization of migration is also a result of the increasing demand for care work, which is often provided by migrant women.

The largest driver of the phenomenon is the crisis stemming from the various effects of climate change and environmental degradation. Climate change affects the livelihoods of people living in rural areas, particularly those who depend on agriculture and fishing. In many cases, these communities are forced to migrate due to drought, loss of crops, lack of water for livestock, and other impacts of climate change. Women are often the primary caregivers and are responsible for ensuring that their families have access to food and water.

Women who migrate do so for a variety of reasons, including economic necessity, family reunification, and the desire to improve their lives and those of their children. Climate change is one of the factors that can lead to migration, as it can disrupt traditional livelihoods and force people to seek new opportunities elsewhere. Women are often the primary caregivers in their families, and they may migrate to find better jobs or to support their families.

A 2017 study published by the World Food Program analyzed data on migrants from El Salvador, Guatemala, Honduras, and Mexico. The study found that 84% of the migrants had experienced at least one climate-related event in the past year, such as droughts, floods, or hurricanes. These events have led to a decrease in agricultural productivity, which has in turn led to an increase in migration.

The study also found that 65% of the migrants had experienced food insecurity in the past year. This is a significant finding, as it highlights the link between climate change and food security. The study also found that 42% of the migrants had experienced water scarcity in the past year. This is another important factor to consider when looking at the impact of climate change on migration.

The study also found that 35% of the migrants had experienced a loss of income due to climate-related events. This is a significant finding, as it highlights the economic impact of climate change on migration. The study also found that 28% of the migrants had experienced a loss of property due to climate-related events.

The study also found that 17% of the migrants had experienced a loss of livelihood due to climate-related events. This is a significant finding, as it highlights the impact of climate change on the ability of people to earn a living. The study also found that 15% of the migrants had experienced a loss of access to basic services due to climate-related events.

The study also found that 13% of the migrants had experienced a loss of cultural identity due to climate-related events. This is a significant finding, as it highlights the impact of climate change on the cultural fabric of communities.

The study also found that 10% of the migrants had experienced a loss of social cohesion due to climate-related events. This is a significant finding, as it highlights the impact of climate change on the social fabric of communities.

The study also found that 8% of the migrants had experienced a loss of political stability due to climate-related events. This is a significant finding, as it highlights the impact of climate change on political stability.

The study also found that 6% of the migrants had experienced a loss of human rights due to climate-related events. This is a significant finding, as it highlights the impact of climate change on human rights.

The study also found that 4% of the migrants had experienced a loss of access to education due to climate-related events. This is a significant finding, as it highlights the impact of climate change on access to education.

The study also found that 2% of the migrants had experienced a loss of access to healthcare due to climate-related events. This is a significant finding, as it highlights the impact of climate change on access to healthcare.

The study also found that 1% of the migrants had experienced a loss of access to housing due to climate-related events. This is a significant finding, as it highlights the impact of climate change on access to housing.

# The challenge of high-risk sex of adolescents

The majority of the adolescent population is engaged in the practice of casual sex. It is very important to consider thoroughly the impact that this type of behavior has on the health and well-being of young people. The risk of sexually transmitted infections (STIs) is particularly high among teenagers, who are often unaware of the multiple forms of transmission of these pathogens or are ignorant about the risks associated with unprotected sex. Among the most visible symptoms of a rapid increase in STI rates is the growing number of cases of chlamydia and gonorrhea. These problems are exacerbated by the fact that many young people do not seek medical care for their sexual health issues, which can lead to serious complications such as infertility and pelvic inflammatory disease (PID). The spread of HIV/AIDS among adolescents is also a major concern, as it can have devastating consequences for both individuals and families. ## Gender and Social Inequality is a Key Factor in Environmental Justice

Gender and social inequality is a key factor in environmental justice. They have a real participation also because they are not hard laborers.

### Women's Risk and Vulnerability And Adaptive Capacity to Climate Change

Women's risk and vulnerability and adaptive capacity to climate change has been studied widely. Studies show that women are more vulnerable to the adverse effects of climate change than men. This is due to their lower economic status, lack of access to resources, and limited mobility. Women are also more likely to be affected by natural disasters such as floods, droughts, and hurricanes.

Among the most recent and important findings of climate change research, the El Niño phenomenon that occurred in 1997-1998 is notable. This event caused a severe drought in East Africa, which resulted in a famine that killed hundreds of thousands of people. The impact on women was particularly severe because they were responsible for finding food and water for their families.

Several climate change studies have been conducted to better understand the impact of climate change on women. Droughts have increased caused a decrease in crop yields. This has affected the livelihoods of many women who depend on agriculture for their income. The impact of climate change on women has been documented and its quality has deteriorated. On the other hand, women have also shown resilience to climate change by adapting to new ways of farming and finding new sources of water.

The few studies that exist on gender and climate change focus on women's environmental degradation and destruction with women during both consultations show that environmental degradation and the effects of climate change are more severe for women. This is due to their lower economic status, lack of access to resources, and limited mobility. Women are also more likely to be affected by natural disasters such as floods, droughts, and hurricanes.

Identifying and taking into account the differentiated impacts of climate change on women is key to understanding how gender inequality affects environmental justice. This is an important step in addressing this problem. Among the effects highlighted during consultations of the different ways in which women are affected by climate change.

- **Migration**: Women migrate more with health concerns, generating greater poverty. Women have more work because they need to produce food and water. The impact on women's livelihoods has been documented and their vulnerability is higher.
- **On the other hand, consultations indicated that women also included how to reduce risks and adapt to climate change. Women are also more likely to be affected by natural disasters such as floods, droughts, and hurricanes.**
- **Negative impacts in agriculture and fishing for households, especially for women.**

Most of the areas of the world where environmental degradation is most severe are inhabited by women. Indigenous peoples' dependence on natural resources makes them particularly vulnerable to climate change.

# Education and Adolescence

## Increased information flow

Reading, television, and the Internet have increased the flow of ideas, the use of contraceptives, and the rate of sexual activity. This has led to earlier sexual debuts, more premarital sex, and an increase in the number of children born out of wedlock.

### Health risks for adolescents

Adolescents are at risk for sexually transmitted diseases (STDs), including HIV/AIDS, and for unintended pregnancies. They may also be at increased risk for other health problems, such as the negative effects of drug use and violence.

## Family structure

This issue is influenced by the family. The loss of a family member from an accident or death can have devastating consequences on the family's ability to cope with the stress of raising children. Women who are left as sole parents of families face additional challenges, such as the need to find employment and to provide for their children.

### Disintegration of families

Another factor is that many families are no longer intact. Divorce rates have increased, and more children live in single-parent households or with both parents working outside the home. This has led to an increase in the number of children who are left alone after school.

## Migration and displacement by natural disasters

The World Disaster Report 2013 states that women and girls are at risk during migration and displacement due to natural disasters. Women's safety is at risk in addition to other factors such as poverty, lack of access to health care, and the increased risk of sexual violence.

### Overcrowded family homes

Women say that with the increase in family size, there has been an increase in the number of children who are left alone after school. This has led to an increase in the number of children who are left without supervision. There is a need for more support services for these families. The government needs to provide more resources to help these families.

### Migration and displacement by natural disasters

The World Disaster Report 2013 states that women and girls are at risk during migration and displacement due to natural disasters. Women's safety is at risk in addition to other factors such as poverty, lack of access to health care, and the increased risk of sexual violence.

### Overcrowded family homes

# United Nations Conference on Environment and Development

## The human dimension of global environmental change

### United nations water for health

United nations water for health was launched in January 2010 as a direct response to the call by world leaders at the UN high-level meeting on water and sanitation in March 2005. This call recognized that access to safe drinking water and basic sanitation is essential to human health, well-being and dignity, and is critical for the achievement of internationally agreed development goals, including the Millennium Development Goals.

### United nations water

United nations water has been established as a mechanism to provide a platform for intergovernmental policy dialogue, advocacy and partnerships on water and sanitation issues. It seeks to enhance the coordination of efforts to make every drop count towards achieving internationally agreed water related goals and targets, including those contained in the Millennium Declaration.

### The water goal

The water goal is ultimately grounded not by water's role as a commodity but by its importance to human life. To ensure or to control water is to deny people the means to live. Water is essential for agriculture and industry, and it is also necessary for health and well-being. The water goal is to provide safe drinking water and basic sanitation for all people by 2015.

### Impact on women's health

The impact of women's health is significant and often unacknowledged in discussions about the environment and development. Women are the primary users of water and fuel, and they are responsible for collecting water and firewood. They also bear the burden of caring for sick family members. In many cases, women are not recognized as farmers in their country's statistics, which means that their contributions to agriculture are not counted.

### Chronic diseases

Chronic diseases also cause significant morbidity and mortality among women. These diseases include heart disease, stroke, cancer, chronic respiratory diseases, and diabetes. Women are more likely than men to suffer from these conditions, and they are often the primary caregivers for family members with chronic illnesses.

### Last of the land a changing landscape in women and children

Last of the land a changing landscape in women and children. But changes have been slow to come. The impact of environmental change on women and children is significant. Women are often the primary caregivers for their families, and they are also responsible for collecting water and firewood.

One phenomenon that is occurring in some ways is what people refer to as a "feminization" of poverty. This means that more women than men are living in poverty.

# CONCLUSIONS

The high priority caused by the negative effects of climate change to which women and girls are more exposed has led to a greater participation in decision-making processes related to family farming. The feminization of agriculture is very significant and is exacerbated by the lack of access to productive resources, services and infrastructure for agricultural production, livestock and fisheries.

Women face discrimination in access to resources and control of land and property. During the long periods of drought, women are responsible for ensuring the food security of their families through the means of production and generating additional income. It is a way of family life also to assist during the planting season, harvesting and marketing of agricultural products. Women play an important role in the management of natural resources such as water, soil and forests.

Women and girls have been able to assume primary responsibilities for the care of children and the elderly, as well as for the maintenance of the home. This has increased their workload and has made them more vulnerable to health problems. The lack of access to adequate health services and the high cost of medicines make it increasingly difficult to ensure the well-being of the family.

The risk of the situation is aggravated by the imbalances of power and structural inequality between women and men, which are exacerbated in times of crisis such as droughts. This has led to an increase in gender-based violence and a lack of access to justice for victims.

Women's participation in decision-making processes related to family farming is very important and should be encouraged. The development of policies that promote the empowerment of rural women is essential to ensure their full participation in society.

**Page 165**

# Gender violence cannot be detached from the impacts associated with climate change

Gender violence cannot be detached from the impacts associated with climate change. It operates as a multiplier of existing vulnerabilities and is exacerbated by environmental degradation and disruption to social cohesion. The effects of violence against women and girls are amplified in contexts where poverty, gender inequality, and lack of access to justice prevail. These factors are compounded when women are displaced due to the effects of climate change.

The impact of climate change on women's lives is influenced by their roles within households and communities. Women are often responsible for food security, water collection, and care for children and elderly family members. When these responsibilities are disrupted by climate-related events such as droughts or floods, women face increased risks to their health and safety.

The effects of the migration project can be exacerbated by the effects of climate change during this journey, which can lead to an increase in violence against women, such as rape and sexual exploitation. This is compounded by the lack of access to justice and support services for survivors of gender-based violence.

Other forms of violence caused by poverty experienced by families that are increasing due to the effects of climate change are those perpetrated by men and adolescents. Girls are undermined and face increased risks of early marriage, sexual exploitation, and trafficking. This is exacerbated by the lack of access to education and economic opportunities for girls in their communities.

Lack of access to quality and safe health services, as well as their difficult access because of poor infrastructure, increases the risk of maternal mortality and morbidity. The lack of access to reproductive health services and sexual and gender-based violence (SGBV) is exacerbated by the lack of access to justice and support for survivors. This is compounded by the lack of access to quality education and economic opportunities.

There is a particular challenge in strengthening health and protection services, as well as how to respond to the needs of women and girls who are at risk of violence due to climate change. This includes addressing the psychological requirements to be met by adolescents with natural disasters and in coordination with the community. The lack of access to quality education and economic opportunities for women, maternal health, prevention, care, safety, and protection have been exacerbated by a lack of access to justice.

Women, men, boys, girls, and families are not adequately prepared or supported to respond to the impacts of climate change. This is compounded by the lack of access to quality education and economic opportunities for women and girls in their communities.

# Additional Issues of Concern

## Traditional Practices of Female Genital Mutilation

Traditional practices of female genital mutilation continue to be widely practiced in many countries despite the harmful effects and serious health risks involved.

### There are areas where the boy child is favored, but not the girl child

There are areas where the boy child is favored, but not the girl child. In some parts of Africa, for example, a woman's status is determined by her husband's wealth and position in society. This has led to the neglect of women's education and economic empowerment.

### Literacy is also important

Literacy is also important as it enables women to read, write their names on legal documents, and participate fully in the development process. It helps them to understand their rights and responsibilities, and to make informed decisions about their lives.

### Libraries are important

Libraries are important as they provide women with access to information and knowledge that can help them to improve their lives.

### To promote women's participation in decision-making spaces

To promote women's participation in decision-making spaces, individuals surveyed mentioned that it is important for women to be educated and have the skills necessary to participate effectively. It is also important to ensure that they are not excluded from political processes or denied access to information.

### The importance of traditional practices

The importance of traditional practices of female genital mutilation has become increasingly recognized as an important factor in promoting gender equality and empowering women. In many countries, the practice has been banned by law, but it continues to be widely practiced despite the risks involved.

### Historical context

Historically, the practice has been seen as a way to control women's sexuality and ensure their fidelity to their husbands. It is also seen as a way to ensure that women are able to bear children and support their families.

## 3.1 METHODOLOGICAL APPROACH

The paper presents a mixed-method approach that combines both qualitative and quantitative processes. The desk review was conducted by a regular gender specialist with the support of local consultants and experts in the region, who provided information on the current situation. A literature review was conducted to include the main actions taken at the regional level to promote women’s empowerment and gender equality. This includes an analysis of the social protection mechanisms with specialized data for each country in the region, including the results of the 2018 Gender Equality Index (GEI).

Furthermore, an initial consultation activity was conducted with potential end-beneficiaries in Colombia, Costa Rica, Panama, and Peru to validate the findings. The results of this consultation can be found in Annex 7. A stakeholder engagement plan was defined with the goal

# Part II: Gender Action Plan Template (Project/Programme Level)

## §1 Introduction

The Gender Action Plan (GAP) forms the basis for operating the results and recommendations of the Gender Equality Audit (GEA). It aims to ensure that gender equality is integrated into all programme activities, including planning, implementation, monitoring, evaluation, and reporting. The plan will be developed in consultation with relevant stakeholders and will be reviewed regularly to ensure its effectiveness. The plan will also contribute to the achievement of gender mainstreaming and integration of a transformative gender perspective into the programme activities.

The Gender Action Plan is aligned with the [United Nations Entity for Gender Equality and the Empowerment of Women (UN Women) Strategic Plan 2018-2021](https://www.unwomen.org/en/about-us/where-we-are/worldwide/strategic-plan-2018-2021).

To strengthen the Enabling Barrier Adaptation, and strengthening confidence at the community level, the programme will focus on the following key areas:

- **Gender Equality Audit (GEA)**: Conducting a GEA to identify gender equality issues.
- **Decision-making at all levels**: Enhancing women's participation in decision-making processes.
- **Transforming gender relations**: Addressing unequal power relations and promoting gender equality.
- **Enabling environment**: Creating an enabling environment for gender equality.

The programme will contribute to a greater equality through promoting exchanges among women and men, and by strengthening the capacity of local institutions. It will also promote the development of gender-sensitive policies and practices, as well as those that may exist regarding gender and social inclusion.

## §2 Impact Statement

The programme's objective is to increase climate-resilient sustainable development by facilitating women's leadership in the field and later affect other communities. The key benefits of this programme include:

- **Empowerment**: Increased empowerment of women.
- **Leadership**: Development of local leaders.
- **Community Engagement**: Strengthening community engagement.

The programme aims to increase and strengthen the involvement of vulnerable communities, especially women and youth, in the By-Countries of Central America and the Caribbean. It will also contribute to the achievement of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) related to gender equality and women's empowerment. The programme will focus on the following key areas:

- **Women's Leadership**: Empowering women leaders.
- **Youth Engagement**: Engaging youth in climate action.
- **Community Resilience**: Building community resilience.

## GAP Budget

The programme’s **GAP** will be executed in all components in the design, implementation, planning and monitoring of activities to ensure that the objectives are achieved.

Persons from the affected population will have several avenues available to seek protection. This includes access to information on how to report abuse and violence, written communication to the extent possible, will establish channels that require face-to-face contact and can provide confidential counseling.

An estimate budget is included in the GAP based on the following methodology:

- 100% of regional gender expert’s budget,
- 50% of national coordinator’s budget and sub-national manager’s budget,
- 30% of regional manager and national coordinator’s budget,
- 20% for new areas where the project has not been implemented in the past.

The estimated budget is based on a conservative estimate. This estimate will require further consideration during implementation and is provided for information purposes only.

# Implementation strategies to increase gender equality in the criminal justice system

## Process of this study

This study was conducted by the Office against Gender-based Violence and Domestic Violence (OFAM) with technical assistance from UN Women and the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP). The study aimed to identify the main challenges faced by women and men in the criminal justice system, as well as the opportunities for their empowerment.

### Activity 1.2: Strengthen the gender action plan

#### Objective
Strengthen the gender action plan that addresses the recommendations of the national human rights commission on gender equality in the criminal justice system.

#### Specific activities include:
- **a)** Ensure the participation of women and men from different sectors, including civil society organizations, in the development of the action plan.
- **b)** Specify activities to ensure the implementation of the action plan.
- **c)** Monitor and evaluate the progress of the action plan.

### Indicators

| # | Indicator Description |
|---|------------------------|
| 1 | Number of consultations held with women and men from different sectors, including civil society organizations, to develop the gender action plan. |
| 2 | Number of training sessions conducted for women and men in the criminal justice system on gender equality and gender mainstreaming. |
| 3 | Number of training programs developed for women and men in the criminal justice system on gender equality and gender mainstreaming. |

### Baseline (2019)

| Indicator | Baseline Value |
|----------|----------------|
| 1        | 50 consultations |
| 2        | 40 training sessions |
| 3        | 30 training programs |

### Targets

| Year     | Target Value |
|----------|--------------|
| 2020     | 60 consultations |
| 2021     | 50 training sessions |
| 2022     | 40 training programs |

### Results

| Year     | Result Value |
|----------|--------------|
| 2020     | 70 consultations |
| 2021     | 60 training sessions |
| 2022     | 50 training programs |

### Accomplished activities

- **a)** Consultations with women and men from different sectors, including civil society organizations.
- **b)** Development of training programs for women and men in the criminal justice system on gender equality and gender mainstreaming.
- **c)** Monitoring and evaluation of the progress of the action plan.

### Challenges

- Lack of resources to conduct consultations with all relevant stakeholders.
- Limited participation of men in the training sessions.
- Difficulty in ensuring the sustainability of the action plan.

### Strengthen Institutional Arrangements
- Implement local administrative areas target large departments in the city center and its zones.
- The need for a gender policy framework that guides the development of gender-sensitive policies, programs, and activities.
  - Specify objectives and include gender equality as an integral part of the organizational structure.
  - At the leadership and team levels, ensure that gender equality is integrated into all aspects of work.
    - Include gender equality in performance management systems.

# Implementation strategies to achieve effective Cancer Control

## Activity 2.1: The focus of the general action plan should be on strengthening and sustaining the capacity of health systems to deliver evidence-based interventions and to promote and sustain efficient technology transfer and uptake.

### Specific actions and include:

1. **Strengthen health systems and infrastructure**
   - Improve the quality of health services.
   - Enhance monitoring & evaluation tools for cancer control.
   - Strengthen health information systems.
   - Ensure adequate human resources, including trained personnel in cancer care.

2. **Integrate cancer into existing health systems**
   - Incorporate cancer prevention and treatment into primary healthcare.
   - Integrate cancer services with other chronic disease programs.
   - Promote collaboration between different levels of the health system to ensure effective coordination and delivery of cancer control activities.

3. **Promote evidence-based interventions**
   - Implement evidence-based guidelines for cancer diagnosis, treatment, and care.
   - Encourage the use of best practices in cancer management.
   - Ensure that healthcare providers have access to up-to-date information on new treatments and technologies.

4. **Strengthen health systems infrastructure**
   - Improve the availability and quality of diagnostic equipment and supplies.
   - Enhance the capacity of laboratories to perform accurate and reliable tests for cancer diagnosis.
   - Ensure adequate funding for cancer control programs.

5. **Promote collaboration with other sectors**
   - Encourage partnerships between government, non-governmental organizations (NGOs), and international agencies to support cancer control efforts.
   - Foster collaboration with academic institutions to promote research and development in cancer control.
   - Promote public-private partnerships to enhance the availability of resources for cancer control.

## Number of simulations that should be conducted to assess the effectiveness of interventions

### Specific actions:

1. **Number of simulations**
   - Conduct a sufficient number of simulations to ensure reliable results.
   - Use different scenarios and assumptions to test the robustness of the model.

2. **Percentage of parameters**
   - Determine the percentage of parameters to include in sensitivity analysis.
   - Include key parameters that significantly affect the outcomes of the simulation.

3. **Sensitivity analysis**
   - Perform a comprehensive sensitivity analysis to identify which parameters have the greatest impact on the results.
   - Use different methods for sensitivity analysis, such as one-way and multi-way sensitivity analyses.

4. **Consistency checks**
   - Conduct consistency checks to ensure that the model is internally consistent.
   - Verify that the results of the simulation are reasonable and consistent with known data.

5. **Validation**
   - Validate the model using external data sources to ensure that it accurately reflects real-world conditions.
   - Compare the results of the simulation with observed data to assess the validity of the model.

## Methods for implementing and evaluating interventions

### Specific actions:

1. **At least two simulations**
   - Conduct at least two simulations to compare different scenarios or assumptions.
   - Use different assumptions about the effectiveness of interventions, the costs of interventions, and other key parameters.

2. **Sensitivity analysis**
   - Perform a sensitivity analysis to identify which parameters have the greatest impact on the results.
   - Use different methods for sensitivity analysis, such as one-way and multi-way sensitivity analyses.

3. **Consistency checks**
   - Conduct consistency checks to ensure that the model is internally consistent.
   - Verify that the results of the simulation are reasonable and consistent with known data.

4. **Validation**
   - Validate the model using external data sources to ensure that it accurately reflects real-world conditions.
   - Compare the results of the simulation with observed data to assess the validity of the model.

5. **Documentation**
   - Document all assumptions, methods, and results of the simulations.
   - Ensure that the documentation is clear, concise, and accessible to others who may need to use or build upon the work.

# Empowerment through Knowledge Sharing in the Context of Community-Based Natural Resource Management

## Table 1: Key Elements of Knowledge Management for Community-Based Natural Resource Management

| Key Element | Description |
|-------------|-------------|
| **1. Knowledge Generation** | The creation, sharing and use of new and existing information, ideas, and insights to meet the problems and needs of community members. This includes: <br> - Gathering knowledge from various sources such as local communities, experts, and research findings. <br> - Encouraging participation in knowledge generation activities through workshops, meetings, and other forums. |
| **2. Knowledge Exchange** | The process by which information is shared among individuals or groups to facilitate learning and problem-solving. This includes: <br> - Facilitating the exchange of best practices and lessons learned between communities. <br> - Encouraging the use of local knowledge in combination with scientific knowledge. |
| **3. Knowledge Application** | The use of knowledge to inform decision-making and solve problems within a community. This includes: <br> - Incorporating new knowledge into existing practices and policies. <br> - Using knowledge to develop innovative solutions to community challenges. |

## Table 2: Key Indicators for Monitoring Progress in Knowledge Management

| Key Indicator | Description |
|--------------|-------------|
| **1. Number of Research Products** | The number of research products that have been produced and disseminated to communities. This includes: <br> - Research reports, case studies, and best practice guides. <br> - Publications in local languages to ensure accessibility. |

### Summary of results

- **Indicator 1**: Press releases issued increased from 0 to 40 over five years.
- **Indicator 2**: Community-based activities increased from 0 to 20 over five years, with gender-specific content increasing from 3 to 23.
- **Indicator 3**: Educational campaigns increased from 0 to 28 over five years, with local languages and women's & children's health content showing similar trends.
- **Indicator 4**: Other activities increased from 0 to 24 over five years, with gender-specific content increasing from 3 to 22.

# Implementation strategies to increase screening rates for cervical cancer

## Activity 4.3

The objective under this activity will be to develop and adapt training materials and support facilities for use by the national cervical cancer control program. These materials will be developed through the engagement of key stakeholders in the development process.

### a) Develop and adapt training materials and support facilities
- Training materials and support facilities will be adapted from existing sources.
- The materials will include information on how to conduct screening, treatment, and follow-up for cervical cancer.
- The materials will also include information on how to use the national cervical cancer control program's resources.

## Implementation and sustainability

### b) Integrate and institutionalize the national cervical cancer control program
- Integrate the national cervical cancer control program into existing health systems.
- Institutionalize the national cervical cancer control program by ensuring that it is included in national policies and guidelines.
- Ensure equal access for rural and remote populations to screening, treatment, and follow-up services.

### c) Ensure equal access for rural and remote populations
- Ensure equal access for rural and remote populations to screening, treatment, and follow-up services.
- Ensure that the national cervical cancer control program is integrated into existing health systems.
- Ensure that the national cervical cancer control program is included in national policies and guidelines.

## Integration of gender equality

### d) Integrate gender equality principles
- Integrate gender equality principles into the national cervical cancer control program.
- Ensure that the national cervical cancer control program is inclusive of all genders.
- Ensure that the national cervical cancer control program is culturally sensitive.

## Monitoring and evaluation

### e) Monitor and evaluate training materials and support facilities
- Monitor and evaluate the effectiveness of the training materials and support facilities.
- Evaluate the impact of the training materials and support facilities on the knowledge, skills, and attitudes of health care providers.
- Collect data on the use of the training materials and support facilities.

## Integration of gender equality

- Ensure that the national cervical cancer control program is inclusive of all genders.
